# Delivery 8 — lot briefs

The brief each lot was built from (`docs/DELIVERY-8-PLAN.md` §1). Item scopes come from the triage and its adversarial check; the defaults are in the plan §2.

## `audit-i18n-shell` — Append-only audit trail with actor user, French completeness guard and message keys for the activity feed, portfolio-wide search, sidebar, date-offset fix and the 'Transfert' wording

Items: R-168, R-184, R-052, R-151, R-167, R-156. Depends on lots: none. Migration: yes.

COMMON (all lots): start from main-2a2fep after the integrator's preparatory commit; read docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, the decisions cited (docs/DECISIONS.md) and the CURRENT code (inventory line numbers may have moved). EN source strings, FR only in your src/i18n/extra/d8-<lot>.json (never fr-dict.json or index.ts); typed text via dataText / projectLabelText / fieldsText, never t(). Rights via getProjectContext / projectAccessResolver (ctx.isLead, ctx.canEdit, refuseClient); each Server Function validates input and permission server-side, writes through DAL writers in one transaction (re-read state inside it, return values from the callback: transactions may rerun), audits, records activity, revalidatePath. Schema: one `npm run db:generate` migration (+ `drizzle-kit generate --custom` only where stated); never edit an existing migration. Notifications: createNotification({ toPersonIds }) after commit, actor removed, try/catch + logError. Tests cite decision ids; prototype goldens are not regenerated (override with a comment) unless stated; put new tests in new files; loop over PERSONAS instead of counting 8. Never edit docs/reste-a-developper.json: write docs/delivery-8/notes-<lot>.md. Insert new code next to related code, never at the end of a shared file. Hand over green: typecheck, lint, test, db:check. THIS LOT MERGES FIRST (its trigger and completeness test then guard every later merge).

R-168 audit hardening, infrastructure (spec 11 §3.12). schema/platform.ts: audit_entries gains actor_user_id uuid (FK users.id, nullable, NO ACTION), before jsonb, after jsonb, reason text, message_key text, message_params jsonb; its project_id FK becomes NO ACTION; activity_events gains message_key text and message_params jsonb (R-184) — all in ONE generated migration. Then a custom migration (drizzle-kit generate --custom, 0012 precedent) that, in this order, backfills actor_user_id from actor_person_id via users.person_id, then creates the plpgsql function, the BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE row trigger and the BEFORE TRUNCATE statement trigger; add a migration test re-running it like migration-0012.test.ts. seed/platform.ts sets actorUserId on the 8 seeded AUDIT rows at insert (userByRole or person → user), since rows can no longer be updated. helpers.ts: AuditInput gains actorUserId?, before?, after?, reason?; writeAudit resolves the user from actorPersonId first, else findPerson(actorName) → users; system actors (System, Lucca, Codex, Cursus, Vigie, connectors, jobs, 'Keystone') keep null; the Entra sign-in audit rows ('Sign-in' area) pass the user id. The change text stays byte-identical (kpis.ts:119-126 and quality.ts:466-467 parse it). Fill before/after/reason only in writers this lot owns (ops-common trace, notifications addAudit, users.ts role/department changes, sign-in); the pass over other modules' writers is a post-delivery sweep (R-168 stays en_cours). listAudit / AuditRow gain reason, before, after and user e-mail; auditCsv (src/domain/admin.ts:306, only that function) adds those columns for the ISO 9001 audit.csv route only; the screen table and the Activity & integrations export keep the prototype's 5 columns; update src/domain/admin.test.ts:228-238. Rewrite src/features/procurement/model.test.ts:317 to insert an earlier-dated 'RFQ sent to …' row with writeAudit({ occurredAt }) instead of an UPDATE (touch only that test). Tests: UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE refused (including a cascaded FK action); user resolved for a demo persona and an Entra user, null for a system actor; reason/before/after stored; seeded rows carry actor_user_id; npm run db:check. Notes: REVOKE waits for a separate table-owner role (I-1a); GDPR purges (R-173) will need a maintenance script disabling the trigger inside one transaction; the trigger is tamper-evidence, not tamper-proof.

### Item scopes (triage, after the adversarial check)

#### R-168 — Renforcer le journal d'audit (avant / après, motif, utilisateur, immuabilité) (buildable, M)

As proposed (items 1-7), with these corrections and additions. (1) Schema: on audit_entries add actor_user_id uuid (FK users.id, nullable, NO ACTION), before jsonb, after jsonb and reason text, and change the project_id FK to NO ACTION. Generate the migration with npm run db:generate (0016). (2) Custom migration 0017. In this order: backfill actor_user_id from actor_person_id via users.person_id, then create the plpgsql function and the BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE row trigger and BEFORE TRUNCATE statement trigger. Add a migration test like migration-0012.test.ts. (3) Seed: src/server/db/seed/platform.ts must fill actorUserId on insert for the 8 AUDIT rows from ctx.userByRole or person → user. On a fresh database the migration backfill sees an empty table, and the rows can no longer be updated after insert. (4) helpers.ts: AuditInput gains actorUserId?, before?, after? and reason?. writeAudit resolves the user from actorPersonId first, then findPerson(actorName) → users. System actors (System, Lucca, Codex, connectors, jobs, 'Keystone') keep null. The Entra sign-in audit rows ('Sign-in' area) pass the user id directly. (5) Reason and before/after in the writers that already hold them. The full file set: lifecycle, purchasing, requests, contingency, baselines, plan-trash, users, hr, schedule, projects-register, changes, change-posting, settings, budget-categories, mtypes (Settings), project-team, rate-card and approval writers, and the wrappers ops-common trace, contract.ts:378/387, meetings-core.ts:19, resources.ts:411, project-team.ts:159 and notifications addAudit. The change text stays byte-identical, because kpis.ts:119-126 and quality.ts:466-467 parse it. (6) listAudit/AuditRow gain reason, before, after and user e-mail. auditCsv (src/domain/admin.ts:306) gets the extra columns; update src/domain/admin.test.ts:228-238. Only the audit.csv route uses them. The screen table and the Activity & integrations export provider (src/server/export/providers/admin.ts:76) keep the prototype's 5 columns. Add the i18n of the new CSV column titles in src/i18n/extra. (7) model.test.ts:317: insert an earlier-dated 'RFQ sent to …' row with writeAudit({occurredAt}), or freeze the clock around createRfq, instead of updating the row. (8) Tests: UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE are refused, including a cascaded FK action. The user is resolved for a demo persona and an Entra user, and is null for a system actor. Reason, before and after are stored. The seeded rows carry actor_user_id. npm run db:check and npm test pass. Document in the delivery notes that REVOKE waits on I-1a and that GDPR purging (R-173) will need a maintenance script that disables the trigger inside one transaction.

Risks: Seed gap. On a fresh database the migrations (backfill and trigger) run before seedIfEmpty, so the 8 seeded AUDIT rows keep actor_user_id null unless src/server/db/seed/platform.ts sets it at insert. They cannot be fixed later with an UPDATE. · Order inside the custom migration matters. The backfill UPDATE must run before CREATE TRIGGER, or the migration aborts on existing data directories (the desktop Keystone.app PGlite on the owner's Mac, an existing PostgreSQL). · Every future data migration or backfill that rewrites audit rows (vocabulary renames, GDPR anonymisation R-173) is refused. It must ALTER TABLE ... DISABLE TRIGGER inside one transaction as the table owner, so document this. · The trigger is tamper-evidence, not tamper-proof. The table owner or a superuser can disable it or set session_replication_role=replica. Real immutability needs a separate owner role plus REVOKE, which depends on hosting (I-1a). · Resolving the user from actorName via findPerson matches fullName OR shortName (helpers.ts:17-23), so an ambiguous short name could attribute an entry to the wrong user. Prefer actorPersonId, or pass the viewer's userId from Server Functions where available. · The change text must stay byte-identical. kpis.ts:119-126 matches 'status: Draft → Open' and 'RFQ sent to %', and quality.ts:466-467 matches 'status: Submitted → '. Moving the reason out of lifecycle.ts:195's text into the reason column changes what the screen shows unless the text is kept. · The Files list in the triage scope omits several writers named in item 4: settings.ts, budget-categories.ts, change-posting.ts, mtypes.ts, hr.ts, schedule.ts, projects-register.ts, resources.ts, and the rate and approval writers. It also omits seed/platform.ts and src/domain/admin.test.ts, so the effort is closer to M+ than M. · The generated migration drops and re-adds the project_id FK. drizzle-kit does not model triggers, so later db:generate runs will not drop them. Reviewers must still not hand-edit 0016 (migration rule). · A 1,000,000-row CSV with before/after JSON grows the export size. The CSV-injection neutralisation in csvCell already covers the new cells.

#### R-184 — Contrôler automatiquement la complétude de la traduction française (buildable, L)

A. Completeness checks (vitest), unchanged in substance:
- src/i18n/completeness.test.ts scans src for t('…') / t("…") literals and the static parts of t(`…`) templates. It excludes src/server/prototype, tests, goldens and the dev gallery src/app/dev.
- Each literal needs an exact French entry, or a FR_RULES match whose output changes the whole string (not one segment), or a place on the reviewed src/i18n/same-in-french.json.
- Add the missing translations it finds ('Assigned', 'Action due', 'Client / sponsor', 'Budget (k)', 'of M', 'Total (h)'…).
- Extend src/i18n/extra/dictionaries.test.ts to every dictionary: one translation per key across files, no empty value. It passes today.
- Add a French render test of Admin › Activity & audit and its export.
B. System texts as code plus parameters, for the activity feed first:
- One generated migration adds nullable message_key + message_params jsonb to activity_events.
- src/domain/system-messages.ts maps each key to EN and FR templates with typed parameters:
  - data parameters (names, titles, notes, error text from external systems) are shown with dataText;
  - references are shown as is;
  - dates, amounts and counts are formatted with Intl, with French plurals.
- recordActivity and the trace helpers (ops-common.ts:62 plus the local ones in contract.ts and project-team.ts) accept {key, params} and still fill the English text.
- The readers (features/admin/model.ts plumbingView, export/providers/admin.ts) render by key in the viewer's language. Legacy rows without a key go through fieldsText instead of t().
- Add exact French entries for the 12 seeded ACTIVITY and 8 seeded AUDIT texts so the demo stays French. Update the French golden in features/admin/model.test.ts, citing R-184 / NEW-D1-2, wherever a value changes.
- Order of conversion: first the connector runs and additions (integrations.ts trace, connector-config.ts, site.ts syncCursus, the Vigie, Codex and Cursus integrations). Then every activity text, across all ~49 writer files, not only the 10 listed.
- Audit trail: shown without t(). The generic change shapes (« status: A → B », « added », « removed », « field: old → new ») are rendered at display time by parsing those shapes, with status labels through t() and old/new values as typed. message_key/params on audit_entries go in with R-168 (before/after jsonb, reason), in the same migration and the same pass over the writeAudit call sites. If R-168 is not scheduled in delivery 8, add them here with the same catalogue.
- The English templates keep the exact strings that kpis.ts:123 and quality.ts:468 query, with a test. Notifications are not converted.

Risks: Seeded prototype activity and audit rows have NO exact French entries today (all 20 checked). Switching legacy rows from t() to fieldsText turns the French demo feed and audit back to English and breaks the French golden in src/features/admin/model.test.ts:222-224. Exact entries must be added, or the seed keyed. · Readers match exact English audit text: kpis.ts:123 ('status: Draft → Open', 'RFQ sent to %') and quality.ts:468 ('status: Submitted → '). A catalogue template that drifts from these strings silently breaks the RFQ send-date KPI and the deliverable approval count. · The scope is underestimated. Activity texts are written from about 49 files, with local trace and audit helpers in contract.ts, project-team.ts and resources.ts, plus the integrations and interchange code. The listed files cover about a fifth of them, and the effort is L, not M. · Overlap with R-168 (audit before/after jsonb, reason, immutability, ~135 audit call sites, now unblocked because R-039 is done): two migrations on audit_entries and two passes over 107 writeAudit sites unless the two items are planned together. · Accepting any FR_RULES match in the completeness test lets partial translations pass ("Vigie sync · 2 sent · 0 en attente · 3 refreshed"). The allowlist of words identical in French is larger than claimed: 106 unchanged literals outside src/app/dev, not 48. · The literal scan cannot see t() calls on variables (t(c.statusLabel), t(k.label), domain label tables). The completeness guarantee is partial unless those label tables get their own check. · Mixed rows: a parameter can be text typed in Keystone or error text from Vigie or Cursus (sync run `error`, connector-config `changes.join(' · ')`). Such a parameter must be marked as data and shown as typed (R-148), or the segment translator mangles it again. · A render test asserting "no English template words remain" is heuristic and can be flaky. It needs an explicit word list.

#### R-052 — Étendre la recherche globale au portefeuille, filtrée selon les droits (buildable, S)

As triaged, with these corrections.

1. Staff search, in `searchShell` (src/server/dal/shell.ts):
   - Purchase orders, change requests, NCRs and risks are searched across the portfolio. Each hit links to its own project: `deepLink('orders'|'changes'|'risks', …, hit.projectCode)` and `ncrHref(hit.projectCode, number)`.
   - The project code is added to the sub-line and listed in `subData` (R-148). Add a `searchHitView` test that the code is shown as typed.
   - Do not limit project-bound kinds to `hasData` projects. Read every project the readers return, so CRs, risks or draft POs on projects created with the wizard are not dropped. (Activities keep their existing `hasData` rule.)
   - Within each kind, the current project's hits come first, then the others in register order. Keep the per-kind limits (3).
2. Client search, in `searchShellClient`: change requests of every project of their organisation (the `own` list from `clientRefusedProject`), each opening that project's Changes page. Still no PO, NCR, risk, part, person, ECO, drawing or activity.
   - Put the "which CRs a client sees" rule in one shared predicate. Today it is every CR of the project, the same as the Changes page. A later answer to the cost-control open question on internal CRs can then change search and the page together.
3. Reads:
   - Optional light SQL readers: `searchChangeRequests(q, codes)`, `searchRisks(q, codes)` and a PO search by number or supplier. They must match on `number || ' ' || title` (or supplier) with ILIKE and escape `%`, `_` and `\`, so the results equal today's `has()` substring semantics. Otherwise reuse `listAllPurchaseOrders`, plus per-project `listRisks` / `listChangeRequestDetails` over `listProjects()`.
   - NCRs: one call to the Vigie connector, `listNcrs({ projectCodes })`. The `activityNames` map is not needed for search.
   - Keep every read inside `safe()`, and keep `listProjects` memoised.
4. Update the `searchShell` and `searchShellClient` doc comments.
5. Tests: update shell.test.ts:228, :262-263 and :287 with comments citing X-5. Add:
   - PO-1082 found from NDC-031, with href /projects/HLT-026/orders?po=PO-1082;
   - NCR-029 found from EWA-051;
   - the client finding CRs of another of their own projects, and nothing from another organisation;
   - a staff hit on another project that opens that project.
6. Update docs/reste-a-developper.json (R-052 progress) and the remaining-work page, as the standing rule requires.

Risks: Per-kind limit of 3 with current-project-first ordering: a broad query (e.g. 'ncr', 'cr-0') will often show only current-project hits, which approaches the 'current_first' option the owner did NOT choose. Specific references (PO-1082) work. Consider whether the limit should stay at 3. · ILIKE readers must reproduce the in-memory `has()` semantics: match on the concatenation number + ' ' + title (or supplier for POs), and escape %, _ and \ in the user query. Otherwise hits differ from today's, or a query of '%' matches everything. · Filtering project-bound kinds by `hasData` (as the triage default suggests) would silently drop CRs, risks or draft POs on projects created with the wizard, which have no project_controls row. Query all projects instead. · Client CR exposure: the cost-control open question 4 (notes-cost-control.md §8, 'internal CRs shown to the client', e.g. CR-007) may later restrict which CRs a client sees. Widening client search to all their projects multiplies the exposure if that answer comes back as 'client-approved only'. Use one shared predicate with the Changes page and portal. · Vigie http mode: `listNcrs` triggers a mirror refresh at most once a minute. One multi-project call is fine, but per-project calls in a loop would repeat the refresh check and add latency to every keystroke search. · Existing test expectations change (shell.test.ts:228, :262-263, :287). Each must cite X-5 so reviewers do not read the change as a regression; NCR-04x ordering from NDC-031 may gain hits from other projects. · Performance: `listAllPurchaseOrders` loads every order with its lines; per-project `listChangeRequestDetails` loads approval chains and WBS. Acceptable on the seed, heavy on a real portfolio; the light readers are preferable once data grows. · The client project hit still lacks `subData` for the PM name (pre-existing). Fix it while touching `searchShellClient` so the typed PM name is not passed through t().

#### R-151 — Toujours afficher le nom du groupe dans une section de menu repliée (buildable, S)

In buildSidebar (src/components/app/shell/nav-model.ts), push the 'group' row before the first SHOWN item of its group, not before the group's first gated item. Use `shown.find((y) => y.def.group === g) === x` in place of `gi[0] === x`. Open, bright, badge and count stay as they are, still computed over all of the group's gated items (`gi`). Open sections behave exactly as before. In src/components/app/shell/nav-model.test.ts, add these tests:
(1) role director (or pm), `navOpen: { project: false }`, active 'changes': the Project rows are [group 'Control' (open false, bright true), item 'changes'].
(2) active 'schedule' with Project collapsed: [group 'Steering', item 'schedule'].
(3) the existing default open-section test stays unchanged, including the closed sub-groups that already show their headers.
Do not use the client role to show the fix, because 'changes' is already first in its gated Control group. Optionally update docs/spec/01-shell-navigation-i18n.md §4 (line 246) to cite X-8. After merging, update docs/reste-a-developper.json and the remaining-work page.

Risks: The test example must use a role that sees 'risks' (director or pm). With the client role the header already shows, so the test would pass without the fix. · The group header shown in a collapsed section stays a clickable sub-group toggle. Opening the sub-group there reveals no extra items, because the section filter still applies. This is acceptable but could confuse. · In a collapsed section, the group badge and count still sum all the group's items, including hidden ones. This matches what the header shows when the section is open. · Spec 01 line 246 describes the old behaviour. Update or annotate it with X-8 so later readers don't restore the prototype behaviour.

R-004 backups and restore, OFF unless KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR is set (never on the Mac app otherwise). src/server/backup/snapshot.ts writes KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR/<UTC stamp>/: database first (`pg_dump --format=custom` on PostgreSQL, `db.$client.dumpDataDir('gzip')` on PGlite), then the files listed by that snapshot's stored_files copied incrementally into a shared content store (a file deleted in between and not already stored is listed as missing), manifest.json (time, package version, applied migration count, seed mode, each file's path, sha256 and size). Pure rules in src/domain/backup.ts: backupsToPrune (every snapshot 48 h, then daily for 30 days, then monthly for 12 months) and content-store garbage collection by reference from kept manifests. Job 'backup' (default every 10 min, KEYSTONE_BACKUP_EVERY_MIN, so RPO ≤ 15 min holds; worker on PostgreSQL, in-process on PGlite). `npm run backup` (PGlite only with the server stopped, same guard as the worker) and `npm run restore -- <snapshot> --target <empty DB URL | new data folder>`: refuses a non-empty target, pg_restore --no-owner or loadDataDir, then files, verifies every stored_files row has its file with matching sha256 and the row counts, reports pending migrations and points to db:migrate, prints elapsed time. Monitoring: outcome in job_locks and one JSON log line per run; an error log line when the last good snapshot is older than 30 min (no e-mail). KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR, KEYSTONE_BACKUP_RETENTION, KEYSTONE_BACKUP_EVERY_MIN in parse.ts VARS (next to the storage block) and .env.example; package.json scripts. Tests: pure rules; PGlite round trip (identical DAL reads and file hashes); PostgreSQL variant opt-in through KEYSTONE_TEST_DATABASE_URL. ERROR'; 'INSTREAM size limit exceeded. ERROR' and EPIPE/ECONNRESET while writing = unavailable; connect timeout 5 s, scan 30 s. ### Item scopes (triage, after the adversarial check)

#### R-003 — Host a multi-user instance (beta, then production) (partly buildable, M)

Build on a delivery-8 branch that replaces PR #1. Ask the owner to close PR #1.

1. **Dockerfile** (node:22-bookworm-slim):
   - npm ci with dev dependencies kept, because tsx runs db:migrate and the worker;
   - build args NEXT_PUBLIC_CODEX_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_CURSUS_URL and NEXT_PUBLIC_VIGIE_URL, unset when empty;
   - next build, then remove the build-time .data;
   - ENV NODE_ENV=production, KEYSTONE_DATA_DIR=/data, TZ=Europe/Paris, LOG_FORMAT=json;
   - a non-root user that owns /data;
   - HEALTHCHECK through `node -e fetch('http://127.0.0.1:3000/api/health')`, because the slim image has no curl or wget;
   - EXPOSE 3000, CMD next start -H 0.0.0.0 -p 3000.
   - Declare only the NEXT_PUBLIC_* values as build args. With NODE_ENV=production during the build, the startup check opens the database, so DATABASE_URL and the seed or auth variables must not reach the build.
2. **.dockerignore**: PR #1's list plus .artifacts, test-results, .claude, design, docs, .next-ci and tsconfig.tsbuildinfo.
3. **deploy/compose.yaml** for a Coolify Docker Compose resource:
   - db: postgres:16 with a pg_isready healthcheck and a named volume;
   - migrate: a one-shot `npm run db:migrate` with KEYSTONE_SEED explicit, labelled to be left out of Coolify's health status;
   - app: depends on migrate (service_completed_successfully), with the /data volume;
   - worker: `npm run worker` with the same environment and the same /data volume;
   - one image shared by all three Keystone services.
   - Document the single-container PGlite variant too: no DATABASE_URL, KEYSTONE_JOBS=inprocess, a /data volume.
4. **CI job**: docker build, run the container with PGlite, KEYSTONE_SEED=demo and KEYSTONE_AUTH=demo, wait for /api/health 200, then one page 200. No push.
5. **docs/DEPLOYMENT.md plus a short French README section**:
   - Coolify settings: port, domain, TLS, build variables, environment from .env.example, volumes, and the KEYSTONE_CONNECTORS_FILE mounted read-only;
   - the upgrade procedure: build, migrate, restart, then the health check;
   - the rule that demo mode is never exposed without host basic auth.
   - The beta that works now is the demo seed with KEYSTONE_AUTH=demo behind Coolify basic auth.
   - The Entra ID section (app registration, redirect https://<host>/api/auth/callback, app roles) is documented but marked as waiting. With KEYSTONE_SEED=production nobody can sign in until people exist. That waits for auth open question 1 (a first list of people from the owner, or the Cursus directory import, R-027).
   - State clearly that backups (R-004, I-1b) and off-disk file storage (R-005) are not covered. Keep a "Production target" placeholder until I-1a is decided.

Not buildable now: a beta for real users with Entra and the production seed, and any bootstrap or import of people (D-7a, D-7b, the open auth question).

Risks: Entra plus the production seed gives an instance nobody can sign in to: no persons are seeded and none can be created in the app. This waits for the owner's answer to the open auth question (DELIVERY-7.md §8, notes-auth.md §7 Q1) or for the Cursus API (R-027). · Demo mode ("View as" any persona, including the director) relies only on Coolify basic auth. Nothing in the code enforces it, so a misconfigured resource is open to anyone. The production config only warns when KEYSTONE_AUTH is unset with the demo seed. · Checking in the sandbox is not straightforward: no Docker daemon runs, and npm ci and the Google Fonts download inside a container need the agent proxy (127.0.0.1:35273, --network host, proxy build args and the CA). PR #1's session failed here. The GitHub Actions job is the reliable check, and it only runs on a PR or on a push to main. · R-001 is not done: origin/main is still at delivery 5 (95412b5), 87 commits behind main-2a2fep. Coolify must track the delivery branch until the owner merges. · Changing the model from PR #1's single container to Compose changes the Coolify resource type the owner may already have set up. DELIVERY-7-PLAN.md §3 had left R-003 for the owner's review, so get confirmation before closing PR #1. · No backups (R-004, RPO ≤ 15 min and RTO ≤ 4 h per I-1b) and files stay on a local volume (R-005). Real beta data on the named volumes could be lost. Keep the beta to demo data, or document a manual pg_dump. · Coolify quirks: the one-shot migrate container can mark the stack degraded (it needs the exclude_from_hc label); each service with a build key may build the image several times unless they share one image; runtime variables marked as build variables could reach next build. · The image stays large (full node_modules with dev dependencies, tsx). A bundled worker or standalone output is still an open question (notes-platform-web.md §6, R-006). · HSTS is host-only (an R-008 open question). With the Entra session cookie, the beta host must be HTTPS-only behind Coolify's Let's Encrypt.

#### R-004 — Backups and restore (RPO ≤ 15 min, RTO ≤ 4 h) (partly buildable, M)

Build now — hosting-independent backup and restore, off unless KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR is set.

(1) src/server/backup/snapshot.ts writes KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR/<UTC stamp>/:
- the database: `pg_dump --format=custom` on PostgreSQL, or `db.$client.dumpDataDir('gzip')` on PGlite;
- the files: taken AFTER the database snapshot, from that snapshot's stored_files list, copied incrementally into a shared content store under KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR. A file deleted between the two steps and not already stored is listed as missing in the manifest;
- manifest.json: time, Keystone version (package version or a build-time variable, since no git is available at runtime), applied migration count, seed mode, and each file's path, sha256 and size.

(2) Scheduling:
- a `backup` job in JOB_SCHEDULES with a 10-minute default, so runs stay under the 15-minute RPO. It runs in the worker on PostgreSQL and in-process on PGlite;
- `npm run backup`: on PostgreSQL at any time; on PGlite only with the server stopped (same guard as the worker), otherwise it refuses with a message;
- pure rules in src/domain/backup.ts: `backupsToPrune` (48 h, then daily for 30 days, then monthly for 12 months) and the content-store garbage collection (files referenced by no kept manifest).

(3) `npm run restore -- <snapshot> --target <empty DB URL | new data folder>`:
- it refuses a non-empty target;
- it restores the database (pg_restore --no-owner, or PGlite loadDataDir) and then the files;
- it verifies every stored_files row has its file with a matching sha256, and the row counts;
- it reports pending migrations and points to `npm run db:migrate` rather than failing;
- it prints the elapsed time. On PGlite the server is stopped and the restored folder then replaces the old one.

(4) Tests:
- the pure rules;
- a PGlite round trip with identical DAL reads and the files' sha256;
- a PostgreSQL variant, opt-in through KEYSTONE_TEST_DATABASE_URL (it needs pg_dump and pg_restore at least at the server's major version).

(5) Monitoring:
- the outcome in job_locks and one JSON log line per run;
- an error log line when the last good snapshot is older than 30 min. No e-mail (R-036).

(6) Settings:
- KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR, KEYSTONE_BACKUP_RETENTION and KEYSTONE_BACKUP_EVERY_MIN in src/server/config/parse.ts VARS and .env.example;
- package.json scripts backup and restore.

(7) Runbook as a new docs/OPERATIONS.md, or a README section:
- RPO and RTO targets, and the fact that the RPO is met only once an off-site copy exists;
- what the host must provide: a mounted folder replicated off-site, pg_dump in the worker's environment, the worker sharing the files folder;
- a step-by-step restore and a quarterly drill.

Not now:
- the Dockerfile and postgresql-client in the image: PR #1 / R-003 is unmerged;
- the off-site target and managed PITR (WAL) in production: hosting I-1a.

The scheduler can run the job while R-004 stays "en_cours" until I-1a.

Risks: RPO ≤ 15 min is not actually met while snapshots stay on the same host or volume. Without an off-site copy (I-1a), losing the disk loses the backups too, so R-004 must not be marked 'fait'. · With a 15-minute interval, the possible data loss is the interval plus the run time plus timer drift (minGapMs is 0.9 × the interval). That exceeds 15 min; 10 min or less is needed. · PGlite dumpDataDir reads the whole data folder synchronously on the web server's event loop and holds the full tar plus gzip in memory. Requests stall for the length of each dump, and memory spikes as the database grows. It does not checkpoint, so a restore relies on PostgreSQL's crash recovery replaying the WAL, which must be verified by the round-trip test. · File and database consistency: deleteStoredFile and the plan-trash purge remove files. A file deleted between the database snapshot and the file copy may be absent from the store, so the manifest must record it and the restore check must tolerate it. · Garbage collection of the incremental content store needs reference counting across kept manifests. Otherwise deleted files pile up, or pruning breaks older snapshots. · On PostgreSQL with several web servers, stored files sit on each server's local disk (R-005 is open). The worker sees them only if it mounts the same files folder, otherwise the file backup is incomplete. · pg_dump and pg_restore must be at least the server's major version and present where the worker runs. There is no image in the repo to add them to (PR #1 is open and unmerged), so this stays a runbook requirement. · The Mac desktop app (PGlite, NODE_ENV=production) must not start writing snapshots every 15 minutes: backups must be off unless KEYSTONE_BACKUP_DIR is set. · Storage volume: keeping every snapshot for 48 h means about 192 full database dumps (288 at a 10-minute interval). Disk use must be sized and the retention set. · On PostgreSQL, restoring into an empty database also needs the roles and extensions to exist; pg_restore --no-owner needs testing against a managed server. · The elapsed time measured on the demo data is not proof of a 4 h RTO at production size. The quarterly drill on real volumes is what proves it. · Retention, interval and the 30-minute staleness threshold are defaults the owner has not confirmed. List them in the lot notes and in DELIVERY-8 §8. · Restoring an older snapshot after a deploy leaves pending migrations. The restore must point to db:migrate rather than fail, and the app refuses to start on a PostgreSQL database with pending migrations until it runs.

#### R-174 — Faire auditer la sécurité avant production (test d'intrusion, veille des dépendances) (partly buildable, S)

Build now:
(1) .github/dependabot.yml for the npm and github-actions ecosystems. Weekly schedule; minor and patch updates grouped; next and eslint-config-next kept in the same group because both are pinned to 16.4.0 and must move together; PRs reviewed by the team, never auto-merged. Dependabot security-update PRs are not set by this file: they need the repository's Dependabot alerts and security updates turned on in GitHub settings, which the owner does.
(2) A dependency-audit check, `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`, which fails on high or critical production advisories and only reports moderate ones. Put it in a lightweight separate workflow (or a separate job) that runs on pull_request, on push to main and on a weekly `schedule:`. Scheduling the whole 45-minute CI with end-to-end tests every week would spend the private repository's Actions minutes.
(3) docs/SECURITY.md (GitHub recognises docs/), containing:
- The update policy, written as a default for the owner and IT to confirm: high or critical fixes within 7 days, monthly minor updates, Next.js patch releases tracked.
- How to report a vulnerability, with a placeholder contact until the owner gives one.
- The known advisories: production uuid/exceljs (not exploitable, v4 only), and the dev-tooling highs through eslint-config-next and drizzle-kit.
- The penetration-test scope, ready for the provider: rights by role × project team (R-041), client refusal (R-042), Server Functions, every Route Handler (auth/sign-in, auth/callback, auth/sign-out, import, export/[screen], tasks/[taskId]/files, meetings/[number]/transcript, photos, interchange/mspdi/[code], admin/integrations/audit.csv, health), rate limits, CSP and headers (R-008), the Entra sign-in flow, and the rule that the test runs in KEYSTONE_AUTH=entra, never in demo mode. Also how to set up the test accounts.
(4) The uuid advisory: either an npm "overrides" entry pinned to uuid "11.1.1" (CJS-compatible; not ">=11.1.1"), with the xlsx export and import tests re-run, or a documented risk acceptance in SECURITY.md.
Not buildable now: the external penetration test itself, which needs the hosted beta server (I-1a, R-003) and a provider chosen by the owner. R-174 therefore stays "en_cours", not "fait", after this work.

Risks: The files only take effect once delivery 7 and 8 are merged into the default branch main (R-001, waiting for the owner). Until then GitHub ignores dependabot.yml and the weekly schedule (origin/main has no .github; the repository has 0 Actions runs). · Dependabot alerts and security updates must be turned on in the private repository's GitHub settings by the owner; dependabot.yml alone gives only weekly version updates, not immediate security PRs. · An override `uuid >=11.1.1` would install uuid 14 (ESM-only) under exceljs's `require('uuid')`. Pin to 11.1.1, or accept the risk, which is not exploitable because exceljs uses v4 without a buffer. · The dev dependencies carry 5 high advisories (braces, micromatch, fast-glob through eslint-config-next 16.4.0) that the --omit=dev gate does not catch. Fixing them needs next and eslint-config-next bumped together, and a Next.js bump can change behaviour (AGENTS.md warns of breaking changes). · Scheduling the full CI weekly (e2e, 45 min timeout) uses private-repository Actions minutes; schedule only the audit job. · The update-policy deadlines (7 days, monthly) and the vulnerability-report contact commit the owner and IT; present them as defaults with an open question, not as decided. · npm audit depends on the registry advisory feed, so a new advisory can turn CI red on an unrelated PR. Document how to triage it or allow a time-boxed exception. · The penetration-test scope document must say to test in KEYSTONE_AUTH=entra with real test accounts in the Entra tenant. Those accounts depend on IT (open auth question in DELIVERY-7 §8: who signs in first in production).

#### R-175 — Analyser par antivirus les fichiers déposés dans Keystone (buildable, M)

The triage's scope, items 1–7, with these adjustments.

1. Scanner module (src/server/storage/scan.ts). Adapters 'off' and 'clamav' (TCP host and port, or CLAMAV_SOCKET), over node:net with no new dependency, zINSTREAM with 4-byte big-endian chunks. It parses "stream: OK", "<sig> FOUND" and "... ERROR". It must also treat these as unavailable: "INSTREAM size limit exceeded. ERROR", and a socket reset or EPIPE while it is still writing. Connect timeout 5 s, scan timeout 30 s.

2. Configuration. KEYSTONE_FILE_SCAN, CLAMAV_HOST, CLAMAV_PORT and CLAMAV_SOCKET in parse.ts and .env.example. The production rule copies KEYSTONE_AUTH: an error with KEYSTONE_SEED=production unless the value is set explicitly (clamav, or off); a warning in other production runs; off in development and tests. Messages never print values. Update parse.test.ts:135-136, which expects exactly ['KEYSTONE_AUTH'].

3. Scan before the transaction in attachTaskFile, addPhoto, importTranscript and startImport.
   - Normalise the bytes to a Uint8Array before scanning and mark that same object.
   - storeFile copies an ArrayBuffer into a new Uint8Array (files.ts:150), so the WeakSet marker would be lost. Mark the underlying buffer, or pass a scanned token.
   - Route the unused storeUploadedFile (files.ts:178) through the scan, or remove it.

4. Outcomes.
   - FileRejectedError gains the codes 'infected' and 'scan_unavailable'. The tasks and photos routes answer 422 and 503. The import route and importTranscriptAction show the message, which already works through the write() catch at actions.ts:101.
   - The uploader gets a generic message, without the signature name.
   - Quarantine copy plus a .json sidecar under <dataDir>/quarantine, outside filesRoot. A warning log line and an audit entry (area 'Files'; the area column is free text).
   - French entries in src/i18n/extra.

5. Rescan.
   - A new migration from db:generate: stored_files.scan_status, scanned_at, scan_signature. Existing rows default to 'unscanned'.
   - A daily 'files-rescan' job added to JOB_SCHEDULES and definitions.ts.
   - readStoredFile and streamStoredFile must return a distinct "blocked" result, not null, so the three download routes can answer 410 instead of 404. interchange fileOf must fail as well.
   - Narrowing: purge only quarantine copies of uploads refused at upload time after 30 days. A file that was stored, and that a rescan later flags, is quarantined and blocked but kept until IT deletes it. A ClamAV false positive must not permanently destroy the file of a business record.

6. Tests: a fake clamd on a random port (OK, FOUND, ERROR, size limit, timeout, reset), the EICAR string, the configuration cases, the route status codes, the job, and the marker check.

7. README section for IT:
   - StreamMaxLength of at least 21 MB, and MaxFileSize / MaxScanSize.
   - AlertExceedsMax, otherwise large archives come back OK without a full scan.
   - AlertEncrypted, which refuses legitimate password-protected Office or zip files; leave it to IT.
   - Keep the OLE2 macro heuristics off, otherwise legitimate .xls and .doc files are refused.
   - freshclam signature updates, a clamd sidecar next to Keystone, and port 3310 never used on the Mac.

Afterwards, update docs/reste-a-developper.json and run npm run remaining:page.

Risks: No owner answer asks for scanning. It is a platform-hardening default, like R-008 and R-176. Present it in the DELIVERY-8 notes as a default to confirm, especially the fail-closed choice and the production configuration error. · The production rule (an error with KEYSTONE_SEED=production unless KEYSTONE_FILE_SCAN is set) adds a deployment step for the I-1a beta server: IT must run clamd or set off explicitly. That policy was chosen by Claude, not by the owner, so it must be documented in the README and .env.example. · Fail-closed means a clamd outage blocks every upload with a 503: task files, site and receipt photos, transcripts. Consider adding scanner reachability to the /api/health report, or at least an error log line IT can alert on. · False positives. With clamd's heuristics (OLE2 macros, encrypted archives or documents), legitimate EPC2i .xls, .doc, .zip and .msg files could be refused or later quarantined. Auto-purging rescanned files after 30 days would destroy business records. Keep rescanned files until IT acts. · Archives above MaxScanSize, MaxFileSize or MaxRecursion come back 'OK' without a full scan unless AlertExceedsMax is set. Scanning gives weaker protection than it seems unless IT sets these options. · The WeakSet 'scanned' marker is fragile. storeFile wraps an ArrayBuffer in a new Uint8Array (files.ts:150), so the marker is lost unless the bytes are normalised first. storeUploadedFile (files.ts:178) is an unscanned path today. Existing tests that call storeFile directly are unaffected only while scanning stays off by default. · Returning 410 for flagged files changes readStoredFile and streamStoredFile, which return null today and which the routes map to 404. The return type must change, along with getTaskFile, readPhoto, transcriptFileGate and interchange fileOf. · clamd's protocol edge cases: clamd may close the socket with 'INSTREAM size limit exceeded. ERROR' while Keystone is still writing chunks, so the client must handle EPIPE or ECONNRESET and still read the reply. Replies are NUL-terminated in 'z' mode. · Port clash: clamd's default port 3310 is the Mac smoke-test port (CLAUDE.md). A default of CLAMAV_PORT=3310 with clamav switched on locally would hit Keystone's own smoke server. Tests must use a random port, and no clamd should ever run on the user's Mac. · R-005 (move file storage to object storage, waiting for I-1a) will later replace the local disk. Keep the quarantine and the rescan behind the storage module's API so they can be ported, and avoid raw path logic spread around. · A file a rescan flags still appears in task attachment lists, receipt photo grids and transcripts (hasFile is true), but downloading it gives 410. No new UI state is planned, which follows the no-new-tools rule but may confuse users. The parsed transcript text stays in meeting_transcripts.raw_text. · The import path is behind IMPORTS_ENABLED=false (ch4-defaults.ts:94), so only unit tests can exercise its scan. · The R-016 route matrix and the e2e scenarios do not cover the new 410, 422 and 503 codes. Add route tests, and keep e2e with scanning off. · Standing rule (CLAUDE.md): after the lot, update docs/reste-a-developper.json, run npm run remaining:page and republish the artifact page.

R-088 adjustable hours-variance threshold (R-6b). Settings key 'hours.variance' { pct: integer 1–50 } default 10 (HOURS_VARIANCE_DEFAULT); light cached reader src/server/dal/hours-variance.ts (cache(); fallback 10 when the row is missing or invalid — existing databases have no row) so the hr DAL and pages never import the Settings DAL; seeded by seedSettings; OPTIONAL 'hours' field in SettingsDefaults / parseSettingsDefaults, filled in settingsDefaultsDocument (seed/settings.ts) and fallbackSettingsDefaults, written by resetSettings; setter in src/server/dal/settings.ts (validation, audit and activity via trace) called by setHoursVarianceAction in src/features/admin/actions.ts behind settingsEditor (director only, A-14), then refresh(); Settings view field 'Hours variance threshold (%)' saved on blur or Enter, read-only for non-directors, on its own row (not inside the A-13 sentence), in admin/model.ts SettingsView and the Settings export (src/server/export/providers/admin.ts). The cost-change lot adds the CCB threshold to the same Settings files: insert next to related code, keep fields optional. Domain: hoursVariance(planned, declared, pct) with INTEGER arithmetic — over when v > 0 and v·100 ≥ planned·pct; under when v < 0 and −v·100 ≥ planned·pct; v = 0 → ok; planned 0 with declared > 0 → over; barOver when (declared − planned)·100 ≥ planned·pct; hoursKpis varianceLo uses the same pct (replaces the prototype's 5 %, deviation citing R-6b). Hours page passes the threshold to HoursBody; classes varOver (accent-300) and varUnder (a distinct, less alarming token) with a ▲/▼ glyph or visually hidden text (not colour alone). Tests: hr.test.ts goldens (HLT-026 tones change) with an R-6b comment; boundaries at exactly ±10 %, a non-default pct (7 % on 100 h; 110 h → 121 h for the bar), the 0/0 case; Settings actions (director edits, others refused, out of range refused, Reset restores 10).

#### R-044 — Créer le rôle Finance (contrat, facturation, grille de taux) (partly buildable, M)

Build now:
1) Role plumbing.
- Add 'finance' to RoleKey, ROLE_PRIORITY (after procurement, before member) and ROLE_LABEL ('Finance').
- Entra app role 'finance' / 'Keystone.Finance' and ENTRA_GROUP_ROLES values are then accepted through isRoleKey/roleOfAppRole.
- Add a note in .env.example for IT.
2) Demo persona Léa Marchand, 'Cost controller', department Controls & planning, lea.marchand@epc2i.fr.
- Add her to PERSONAS.
- Create her user apart from the PROTO.PERSONAS loop (organisation.ts:105), with persons.email.
- Add an idempotent backfill for databases seeded earlier. users.role is plain text, so no migration is needed.
- Add FR strings in src/i18n/extra/d8-finance.json.
3) Finance workspace in ROLE_SCREENS.
- Include home, myprojects, docs, overview, cash, contract, billing, budget, hours, rates.
- Because the persona owns activities and tasks (P-9a pattern), also include plan (own-activity % only), mywork/task, leave and meetings.
- Add 'portfolio' only if Finance may score proposals and set weights; otherwise leave it out.
- Home: the director's Home through the default branch of getHomeView.
4) Contract rule (P-4).
- Pure rule canEditContract in src/domain/rights.ts: director, or finance (default: any project, to be confirmed against P-2), or (ctx.isLead AND 'contract' is in the viewer's workspace, so a member-role deputy gets no editing).
- Use it in getProjectContext.canEdit('contract'), in editable() for the contract actions, and in PageHeader (primary and Read-only tag).
- Remove 'contract' from procurement's workspace.
- Update gate.test.ts and the procurement scenario in contract.golden.json, citing P-4.
5) Finance is read-only on Budget, Hours and Billing even when on a project's team. Add a Finance override in getProjectContext.canEdit and in PageHeader showPrimary; this covers category-actions, the hours actions and the billing editable().
6) Rates (P-12 c).
- canSeeRates = director, pm, finance. This covers the task cost note, exports (run.ts:80) and the rates page.
- New canEditRates = director, finance, used in rateEditor, rates/page.tsx canEdit, rates-view and Add role. PMs read the values.
- New refusal and banner wording.
- Change setBaseRate, setMultiplier and addRateRole in dal/hr.ts to take an Actor and re-check the role there.
7) Budget rate (C-9).
- Director and Finance set it: setBudgetRateAction, canSetRate at contract/page.tsx:29, re-check at currency.ts:60.
- New message. Mark it as a default (DELIVERY-7 §8 open question).
8) Margins.
- Decide explicitly and note it as a default: add finance to MARGIN_ROLES (portfolio-finance.ts:9) so Cash flow, Prioritisation, the status report and exports are not masked.
- Supplier prices stay director, pm and procurement (NEW-D2-1).
9) Billing.
- Leave the gate unchanged (director and pm-role team members, via canEdit('billing')).
- Finance reads Billing only.
10) Tests.
- Rights unit tests and gate tests for 9 personas.
- Update auth.test.ts:14, users.test.ts:39/68, shell.test.ts:58/82, home.test.ts (no golden for finance), screens.test, the route matrix with the 9th persona, and the FR dictionary test.

Left for later (owner):
- Finance's rights to issue invoices and mark them paid (P-4 silent; R-182 Sage X3).
- The dated rate grid (R-083 / A-10, R-4a).
- Confirmation that Finance edits contracts portfolio-wide rather than only on its teams (P-2).

Risks: P-2 conflict: letting Finance edit the contract on projects whose team it is not on departs from the named-team rule (P-2 = a), where only the director is exempt. Record it as a default and ask the owner. · ctx.isLead also covers a deputy whose role is not pm (notes-rights §5 Q1). Without a workspace check, canEditContract would let that person edit Contract while the screen is outside their workspace. Gate tests need a case for a member-role deputy. · The persona sits on the NDC-031 and ERP-202 teams. Unless canEdit has a Finance override, she gets edit rights on Budget, Hours, Billing and every other screen of her workspace on those projects. · Léa Marchand owns 16 plan activities, tasks T-2312/T-2325 and request RQ-035. A Finance workspace without plan or mywork hides her own work and goes against the P-9a pattern used for procurement. · Giving Finance 'portfolio' grants writes to 'Score a proposal' and to the weights: pfinance/actions.ts editor() checks only outside. Nobody decided this. · Margins (MARGIN_ROLES = director, pm) would be masked for Finance on Cash flow, Prioritisation, the status report and exports unless that rule is extended too. The triage does not mention it. · The triage says billing 'stays with the director and the project's lead', but today any pm-role team member bills. Moving billing to isLead would be an unrequested change of rights. · The budget-rate setter is an open owner question (DELIVERY-7 §8; notes-contract-portfolio §5 Q3). Letting Finance set it is a default. · The scope's file list is too short. auth.test.ts:14 and users.test.ts:68 assert that 'finance' is not a role. users.test.ts:39 expects 8 accounts. shell.test.ts:58/82 use Record<RoleKey, number>, which breaks typecheck. home.test.ts has a golden per role. The users seed loops over PROTO.PERSONAS (8 entries). · The hr DAL writers (setBaseRate, setMultiplier, addRateRole) take an actorName string. Adding the role re-check changes their signatures and every caller, tests included. · Finance's Home reuses the director's 'Approvals across the portfolio'. Its rows (POs, ECOs, resource requests) link to screens outside Finance's workspace and to approvals Finance cannot make. · In entra mode, IT must create the 'Keystone.Finance' app role or a group mapping. Until then no real user can hold the role. This is not a blocker for the build.

#### R-139 — "View as client" entry for the director and the project's PM (P-11) (partly buildable, S)

Buildable now, without the refusal of other internal roles and without removing the director's raise:

(1) src/domain/rights.ts: `canPreviewClientView(role, access)` = role !== 'client' && (role === 'director' || access.lead), where lead covers the PM of record, co-PMs and deputies. It controls only the entry and the preview banner.

(2) src/components/app/shell/page-header.tsx (+ header.module.css): a secondary "View as client" button with the Eye icon, linking to screenHref('client', {code}).
- Show it in the header actions of project-scoped screens, except the portal itself.
- Show it only when canPreviewClientView holds for the current project (scope.access / scope.isLead are already loaded there).
- The sidebar stays role-based.

(3) src/app/(app)/projects/[code]/client/page.tsx: for the director and the project's leads, a "Client view · preview · read-only" banner with a "Back to project" link replaces the generic "Outside my workspace" banner.
- Keep the PM's `outside`-based hiding of the primary and of edits.
- can.decide and Acknowledge stay client-only, as today.
- The status report keeps today's rule.

(4) Other internal roles keep today's read-only view of the portal outside their workspace (P-1 = a): no redirect, no export 403, route matrix unchanged.

(5) Director's "Raise issue": keep it as today and record it as an open question for the owner. P-11 "en lecture seule" against spec 03 §8 and I-2b = c: today it is the only internal way to record a client issue.

(6) FR strings in src/i18n/extra.

(7) Tests:
- the rights unit test for canPreviewClientView;
- PageHeader / render tests: the entry is visible for the director, the PM of HLT-026 and a deputy; hidden for a team member, a PM of another project, the client, and on the portal itself; the preview banner is shown for the director and the PM;
- the existing rexclient, export and e2e tests stay as they are.

Risks: If the owner later confirms refusing the other internal roles, these must change: the export test that runs every screen for the 'member' persona (src/server/export/providers/providers.test.ts, ~line 56) includes 'client'; the route matrix (all internal personas 200); and the e2e expectations. ExportContext has no `lead` field and the provider `refuse` hook is synchronous, so the check would have to go in buildExportContext. · Removing the director's raise while clients have no access at launch (I-2b = c) would leave client issues with no way in: the R-137 Stakeholders card only takes in charge, answers and closes. · PageHeader is shared by all 59 screens. One more button in the header actions can crowd the tablet layout (R-146, about 900 px): check the header at tablet width. · 'CP du projet' read as isLead also includes co-PMs and deputies, and a deputy whose role is 'member'. On their own team role the director has isLead on every project, so the director gets the entry everywhere, including internal projects with empty portal tabs. · The PM is 'outside' on the portal (ROLE_SCREENS.pm has no 'client'). Swapping the banner must not re-enable the primary action or any write that `outside` hides today. · The notifications 'issue answered / closed' link to the portal's Issues tab for whoever raised the issue (dal/client.ts:268). That link stays valid only as long as the director keeps access and the raise.

#### R-083 — Dater la grille de taux par rôle et unifier la valorisation des heures (partly buildable, L)

Buildable now:

1. Dated versions.
   - New tables rate_role_rates (rate_role_id, effective_from, base_rate_eur, created_by, created_at; unique on role + date) and rate_multiplier_rates (key, effective_from, factor).
   - rate_roles keeps the identity (role, external flag, cost centre, grade as information only, R-4b). Its base_rate_eur and effective_from columns are retired, so there is a single source.
   - The new migration (npm run db:generate plus a backfill SQL file, like 0012) also converts existing databases: each current base_rate_eur and factor becomes a first version dated coalesce(effective_from, '2026-01-01').
   - The seed's grid takes effect on 2026-01-01.
   - An edit or 'Add role' inserts or updates the version for the chosen date. The date is entered explicitly, defaulting to the app's current business date (STATUS_ISO until R-013).

2. One function, rateAt(role, date) and multiplierAt(key, date), in src/domain/hr.ts, with a DAL reader.

3. persons.rate_role_id: a foreign key backfilled by exact job title match. People with no match show 'rate missing' or 'not valued', never an invented rate.

4. Consumers.
   - Hours KPI: Σ per line of (declared − planned) × the person's role rate at the import's period_start. HOURS_VALUATION_RATE is removed.
   - Task cost note and the my.ts export: each time entry at the rate of the person who logged it, on the entry's date. Logged hours with no entry are valued at the rate in effect today. The job_title_rates lookup and the 100 €/h fallback are removed.
   - What-if: people and helpers are valued through their role at the horizon start. Crews and subcontractors are valued through an external role or a grid role. The 60 €/h becomes a clearly named, isolated fallback until the grid has a crew role, rather than dropping the crew's cost.
   - Site weekend premiums: the multipliers in effect.
   - Rate KPIs and the rate-card export: the version in effect.

5. Retire job_title_rates and getJobTitleRates (migration, seed, db-check counts).

6. Rate cards screen: 'since <date>' plus the earlier versions, and an effective-date field on edit. Audit line in the form 'Planner base: 74 → 78 €/h from 1 Nov 2026'.

7. Rights: only the director edits rates and coefficients; PMs keep read access (NEW-D2-1).

8. Tests: hr.test.ts varianceCostK (A-10), actions.test.ts lines 51-58 (P-12), delivery2.test.ts:239,255, delivery2-writes.test.ts:184-191, the whatif.test.ts costs, and production-seed.test.ts (new tables stay empty).

Not buildable now:
- Finance as an editor of the grid (R-044, which waits on P-4).
- Valuing actual cost from Cursus hours (R-074).
- A production source for the person → role link.

Risks: Production has no source for the person → role link. The production seed has no persons and no rate roles, nothing in the code writes persons.job_title, and the Cursus staff directory (R-027) carries matricule, name, e-mail and team, not a role. Until the owner decides who sets a person's rate role, everyone is 'not valued' in production. This should become an open question rather than wait silently for R-027. · What-if regression: if the 60 €/h fallback is simply removed, the Steel erection crew (no linked person, no external role sharing a word) gets no rate. The overtime-on-crew lever's cost then drops to zero or is excluded, and the 'budget' auto-levelling choice changes. Keep a dated crew role or an isolated named default. · The task cost note covers only part of the hours: tasks.logged_h is a stored total, and many seeded tasks have no time entries (T-2302: 4.5 h, none). Valuing per entry needs an explicit rule for the remainder, otherwise cost ≠ logged × rate. A single displayed '€/h' also becomes ambiguous when the rate changes within a task. · Existing databases (./.data PGlite, PostgreSQL) must be converted by the migration, not only by the seed. Keeping rate_roles.base_rate_eur next to the version table would recreate two competing sources. · Default effective date: the app's 'today' is still STATUS_ISO 2026-09-25 (S-4 / R-013 not built), so an edit defaulting to that date revalues the seeded late-September time entries and the September hours import. Require an explicit date and flag this. · Rights interim: taking the edit right from PMs before Finance exists leaves the director as the only person who can edit. That matches P-12 (PMs only consult under c), but the owner should confirm the interim. Finance must be added when R-044 lands, which waits on P-4's invoice rights. · Displayed values change (Hours variance cost, task cost note 95 → 60 €/h for QA / QC, what-if costs). Each test update must cite A-10 / R-4a / P-12. db-check row counts and the production-seed empty-table list must be updated too. · The rates themselves are still prototype demo values. R-178 waits for EPC2i's real grid, and production has no multipliers, with no way to add one.

#### R-178 — Fournir et charger les listes de référence réelles d'EPC2i (partly buildable, S)

Buildable now:
1) Production rate coefficients.
   - Make the production seed (seed/reference.ts) insert the five rate_multipliers rows. Keys and labels are those of the prototype MULTS: ot 'Overtime > 8 h/day', sat 'Saturday', sun 'Sunday / public holiday', night 'Night shift', oncall 'On-call'.
   - Factors default to ×1.25, ×1.5, ×2, ×1.3, ×0.35. The same 1.25 is already the what-if fallback, so this makes it visible and editable. Label the factors in the code comment and in the delivery notes as a placeholder for the direction to confirm (A-10).

Place it in seedIfEmpty before the non-demo early return at index.ts:107, so it also runs on PostgreSQL through db:migrate. Do not put it in a SQL migration: migrations run before the seed, and an INSERT there would make the demo seed's insert into rate_multipliers fail on a fresh database.
   - Add 'rate_multipliers' to REFERENCE_TABLES and keep rate_roles at 0. Add a test that the backfill is a no-op on a seeded database of either mode, and a test that the Site premium and what-if factor read the rows on a production database.

   - It lists each list to supply with its current production default (Settings lists, storage locations, rate roles and coefficients, departments, clients), the constraints, and who validates.
   - Constraints to state: labels unique per list; workflow statuses fixed; holidays written '14 Jul · Fête nationale', with the code being the 2026 date; a location type for each location.
   - Do not present the sheet as an import template (D-7b).
3) Update reste-a-developper.json: R-178 stays 'en_cours' or 'a_faire', not 'fait', with a note.

Not now:
- loading the real lists (waits for the data and for D-7b);
- a department editor or 'add multiplier', which are absent from the prototype;
- any change to 'Reset to defaults' (open question for the owner);
- restricting who edits the grid (R-044 / P-12, Finance role);
- dating the coefficients (R-083).

Risks: Seeding factor values in production decides part of the DELIVERY-7.md §8 platform-data open question ('Reference data of a production start'; notes-platform-data.md §7 Q1 says rates are not seeded). Record it as a default with the open question kept. Show the values as unconfirmed so they are not taken for EPC2i's real coefficients, which A-15 forbids presenting. · Rights mismatch. setMultiplierAction lets PMs edit coefficients in their workspace (features/hr/actions.ts:29, '06 OQ6'). P-12 (c) and R-044 want Finance plus the director, with PMs read-only. Once seeded, the production grid is editable by any PM until R-044 is built. Do not describe it as 'director only'. · R-083 (dated rate grid, A-10 / R-4a) may reshape rate_multipliers, for example with effective dates or history. Sequence the work, or make the seed and backfill go through the same constant, so R-083 does not have to redo them. · Backfill placement. Put it before the early return at index.ts:107, or production databases never get it. It must stay idempotent (onConflictDoNothing on the key primary key) and never overwrite a factor the director edited. A SQL-migration INSERT would collide with the demo seed on an empty database. · The production-seed test asserts the exact set of filled tables and unchanged row counts on reopen. Add rate_multipliers to REFERENCE_TABLES and keep rate_roles at 0. · The collection sheet does not close R-178: the real lists still wait for the direction and for D-7b, and departments stay the prototype's five with no way to edit them in the app. Holiday codes are tied to 2026, so the sheet should ask for dated holidays per year without promising a multi-year calendar. · An .xlsx sheet could be read as a promise of an import, which D-7b defers. Present it only as a data-gathering document.

#### R-086 — Afficher le coût main-d’œuvre du portefeuille par zone sur Ressources (buildable, S)

As proposed, with these adjustments:
1) New DAL reader getPortfolioLaborCost(zone: ZoneKey | null) in src/server/dal/resources.ts. It takes listProjects(), keeps the projects in the zone (inZone) and in execution (inExecution, A-3), and sums budget_keur and actual_keur over every cost_category with costType 'labor' (all of them, not the first). It returns { budget, actual, projects }.
2) Resources page (src/app/(app)/resources/page.tsx): read getZone() and use the reader. Value = money(Σ actual); colour OK; sub-line = `${t(`${share}% of labor budget`)} · ${t(zoneLabel ?? 'All zones')}`, built from the existing FR rule and the existing zone labels. Add an FR entry only if a new string appears. Update the page's header comment (no longer 06 OQ2 behaviour).
3) Model (src/features/resources/model.ts): the portfolio input carries the zone label, and the 'project' scope sub-line stays unchanged.
4) Resources export (src/server/export/providers/resources.ts resourcesWorkbook): use getPortfolioLaborCost(ctx.zone) and add a zone filter line.
5) Project resources and its export keep the project's own labour figure. Prefer switching getLaborCost to costType 'labor' as well, so both screens use one rule; no seeded value changes, since every /labor|staff/ category is typed Labor.
6) Tests: resources-page.test.ts (replace the 'follows the last project' case with zone cases: all zones, euna, afr; the FR value), features/resources/model.test.ts (the portfolio sub-line; missing from the triage's file list), delivery2.test.ts (new reader; displayed-value change cites R-2). Add a note to the delivery doc.

Risks: Population is a choice the owner did not make: R-2 says 'tous les projets de la zone affichée', and the zone bar counts every project in the register (shell.ts zoneBarOf uses listProjects()). The triage narrows this to A-3 execution projects, which leaves out WSA-044 (APD, 160 k€ seeded actual) and any warranty or closed project. That is defensible because the Overview, Cash flow and Prioritisation use A-3, but it is an extension of A-3, and DELIVERY-8 should record it as a default the owner can overturn. · 'La zone affichée' on a global screen: Resources has scope 'global' and no zone bar (page-header.tsx:99 renders the zone bar only for scope 'zone'). The tile follows a cookie that the viewer can only change on another screen, and the heatmap, capacity bars and open requests on the same page stay portfolio-wide. Naming the zone in the sub-line limits the confusion. Turning Resources into a zone screen would be a bigger, unasked change. · Two rules for labour: the portfolio reader matches costType 'labor', while getLaborCost matches the name regex /labor|staff/ and takes only the first category. A project with a second labour-type category, or a renamed one, would give a project figure that does not match its share of the portfolio total. Align getLaborCost with costType. · The value is still the seeded actual_keur column: no Keystone writer exists until R-074 (Cursus hours × dated rate, blocked on an external system). The tile is a correct aggregation of figures that are not yet 'valued hours', the same as Budget. · Visibility: the department head and procurement see Resources and would now see a zone- or portfolio-wide labour cost total, where today they see one project's figure. The owner chose option (a) for this screen, but the total covers projects these roles are not on (R-041 grants no per-project cost rights on global screens). · Test churn: the expected values '€1.42M' / '1,42 M€' and '77% of labor budget' in resources-page.test.ts and model.test.ts change. Under the all-zones execution default they become about '€2.51M' and '70% of labor budget' (2 512 / 3 590 k€). model.test.ts was missing from the triage's file list. · The zone filter depends on the per-project zone (zoneOverride, else the country's zone, else 'euna'). A project without a site falls into EU/NA by default and is counted there.

#### R-087 — Compétences : échelle unique 1–5 et deux indicateurs (buildable, S)

1. R-5a, the form:
   - Remove the 'Levels' field, SkillScale and SKILL_SCALES from ADD_SKILL_FORM and parseAddSkill in src/features/hr/forms.ts. Name and category stay.
   - No schema change: the scale was never stored.
   - In forms.test.ts, change the prototype-shape assertion for AF.skills so it records the removed 'levels' field as a deliberate R-5a departure. Update the parseAddSkill cases.
   - Drop `levels` from actions.test.ts.
2. Domain (src/domain/hr.ts):
   - skillCoverage keeps experts, able and gap (experts < 2), and adds noExpert (experts === 0) and singleExpert (experts === 1).
   - skillKpis returns noExpert and singleExpert as separate counts. With the seed: 2 and 6.
   - Update src/domain/hr.test.ts and cite R-5b.
3. Skills matrix (src/features/hr/skills-view.tsx):
   - Five tiles: Skill families; 'Skills without an expert' (no one at level ≥4); 'Single-expert skills' (exactly one at level ≥4); Certifications; Open requests needing level ≥4.
   - Each of the two new tiles uses LO when its count is above 0.
   - The coverage list still flags every case with fewer than 2 experts, but names which one ('No expert' or '1 expert').
   - Update hr.render.test.ts for EN and FR.
4. Engineering-lead Home (src/domain/home.ts engLeadHome):
   - The watchlist sub-label reads 'No expert — …' when experts = 0 and 'Single expert — …' when experts = 1. The tag stays 'Skill gap'.
   - Add an R-5b overlay for englead in src/server/dal/home.test.ts expected(), for EN and the FR line 259.
   - Update src/domain/home.test.ts.
5. FR translations:
   - Put new keys in src/i18n/extra/ (hr.json, home.json or a new delivery-8 file).
   - Add regex rules in src/i18n/fr-rules.ts for any templated text that contains numbers.
   - Leave src/i18n/fr-dict.json, the prototype dictionary, untouched.
6. Optional: add a 2 / 6 assertion in src/server/dal/delivery2.test.ts.
7. Afterwards: record the deviation in the delivery-8 notes and update docs/reste-a-developper.json per the standing rule.

Risks: The golden test of the engineering-lead Home (src/server/dal/home.test.ts against home.golden.json) will fail unless an explicit R-5b overlay is added. The triage's file list omits this file. · FR translation of the new labels: Home rows go through extra/home.json templates and fr-rules.ts regexes (e.g. /^Single expert — (.+)$/). The coverage text goes through t() and the regex /^(\d+) experts? · (\d+) at level ≥3$/. A new 'No expert…' wording left uncovered would show in English in FR, and the FR tests may not catch every case. · Putting the new keys in fr-dict.json, as the triage proposes, breaks the convention that the prototype dictionary stays untouched. A key translated differently in two dictionaries is caught by extra/dictionaries.test.ts only for d7-* files. · There are two separate skillCoverage functions (src/domain/hr.ts and src/domain/home.ts). Changing only one leaves the Home and the Skills matrix with different definitions of the two cases. · The 'Single-expert skills' tile drops from 8 to 6 with the seed data. Anyone comparing with the prototype or with spec 12 ("8 single-expert skills") will see a deliberate change. Cite R-5b in the tests and the delivery notes. · Five KPI tiles instead of four in KpiGrid min=150: check the tablet layout (R-146) and narrow widths. · Do not add a per-cell level editor or any scale enforcement beyond removing the field. The prototype has none, so that would be a new tool.

#### R-088 — Rendre réglable le seuil d’écart d’heures (±10 %, inclus), couleurs distinctes (buildable, S)

1) Setting. Add app_settings key 'hours.variance' = { pct: integer 1–50, default 10 } with a constant HOURS_VARIANCE_DEFAULT in src/server/db/codes.ts. Read it from a small server module like health-thresholds.ts (e.g. src/server/dal/hours-variance.ts, memoised with cache()) so the hr DAL and pages don't import the Settings DAL. Fall back to 10 when the row is missing or invalid: existing demo and production databases have no row, and the seed (seedSettings) should insert it. Write it through a setter in src/server/dal/settings.ts that validates the value, records audit and activity with trace(), and is called by setHoursVarianceAction in src/features/admin/actions.ts behind settingsEditor (director only, A-14), then refresh(). 2) Reset to defaults. Add an OPTIONAL `hours` field to SettingsDefaults/parseSettingsDefaults (src/domain/settings-defaults.ts). Stored 'settings.defaults' documents were written once with onConflictDoNothing and don't have it; making it required would make them fail parsing and fall back. Fill it in settingsDefaultsDocument (seed/settings.ts) and fallbackSettingsDefaults, and have resetSettings write it. 3) Settings UI. In the Settings view, add an "Hours variance threshold (%)" field saved on blur or Enter, read-only for non-directors. It needs src/features/admin/model.ts (SettingsView) and settings-view.tsx. Put it on its own row, not mixed into the A-13 sentence "One rule everywhere: CPI…", and add it to the Settings export (src/server/export/providers/admin.ts). 4) Domain. hoursVariance(planned, declared, pct) with integer arithmetic: over when v > 0 and v*100 ≥ planned*pct; under when v < 0 and −v*100 ≥ planned*pct; v = 0 → ok (planned 0 with declared > 0 → over); barOver when (declared−planned)*100 ≥ planned*pct. hoursKpis takes the same pct for varianceLo, which replaces the prototype's 5 %; record that as a deviation citing R-6b. 5) Hours page. hours/page.tsx reads the threshold and passes it to HoursBody (a Server Component). Use distinct classes varOver (accent-300) and varUnder (for example accent-500 or neutral-400), with a ▲/▼ glyph or visually hidden text. 6) i18n. FR strings go in the Settings and hr extra dictionaries or fr-dict.json. 7) Tests. Update the hr.test.ts golden tables with a comment citing R-6b; the HLT-026 tones change. Add boundary tests at exactly ±10 % and at a non-default pct (for example 7 % on 100 h, and 110 h → 121 h for the bar), the 0/0 case, and Settings action tests (director edits, others refused, range refused, reset restores 10).

Risks: Float rounding breaks the inclusive boundary if it is coded as planned × threshold or planned × (1 + threshold). In Node, 110*1.1 = 121.00000000000001, so 121 declared on 110 planned is not flagged by the bar. A scan of thresholds 1–50 % found 207 failures (e.g. 7 % on 100 h). Compare in integers: v*100 >= planned*pct. · The proposed rule `declared − planned ≥ planned × threshold` marks planned = 0 / declared = 0 as BOTH over and under (0 ≥ 0). It needs an explicit v = 0 → ok guard. · Existing 'settings.defaults' documents (demo and production databases) lack an hours field. If parseSettingsDefaults makes it required, every stored document fails parsing and Reset falls back to the flagged entries without the stored locations, a silent regression of R-170. · Displayed values change on the seed: under-declarations within 10 % turn neutral (Thomas Kerbrat −8/96, Damien Roux −2/30, WP 1.2 −4/80, WP 1.5 −8/96), and exactly −10 % (Amélie Ollivier) becomes 'under'. Update the golden tests at hr.test.ts:172-200 with an R-6b citation and list the change in the delivery notes. · Replacing the KPI's 5 % with the Settings threshold goes beyond what R-6b says, which is about the declared-vs-planned variance in general. It is a defensible reading but should be noted as a deviation, with the option of keeping a separate KPI rule. The KPI also only flags over-declaration today. · The triage file list leaves out src/features/admin/model.ts (SettingsView), the Settings export provider, and a light DAL reader. Without the light reader, the hr DAL or Hours page would import the whole Settings DAL. · The 'Project health rules' card carries the A-13 sentence 'One rule everywhere: CPI…'. Putting an hours threshold there without separating it may blur that rule. · The Hours screen is still labelled Lucca and will be replaced by Hours (Cursus) (R-030/R-031, blocked). Keep the threshold in the domain function and the setting so the work carries over; don't hard-wire it to the Lucca view. · Owner priority is 'Plus tard' (later), so this item is low priority for delivery 8. · Don't use only colour to tell over from under: the design system has no red, amber or green. Add a glyph or aria text so the two cases differ in colour and shape.

Items: R-069, R-015, R-081, R-077, R-080, R-054, R-140. Depends on lots: none. Migration: yes.

COMMON (all lots): start from main-2a2fep after the integrator's preparatory commit; read docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, the decisions cited (docs/DECISIONS.md) and the CURRENT code (inventory line numbers may have moved). Rights via getProjectContext / projectAccessResolver (ctx.isLead, ctx.canEdit, refuseClient); each Server Function validates input and permission server-side, writes through DAL writers in one transaction (re-read state inside it, return values from the callback: transactions may rerun), audits, records activity, revalidatePath. Never UPDATE/DELETE audit_entries. Tests cite decision ids; prototype goldens are not regenerated (override with a comment); new tests in new files; loop over PERSONAS instead of counting 8. Never edit docs/reste-a-developper.json: write docs/delivery-8/notes-cost-change.md. Insert new code next to related code, never at the end of a shared file. Hand over green: typecheck, lint, test, db:check.
