### Item scopes (triage, after the adversarial check)

#### R-069 — Rendre séquentiel le circuit des CR avec étapes non requises automatiques (partly buildable, M)

Build R-069 as P-6b, P-6c and A-4 say, without the client-portal total, without a CCB rule tied to baselines, and without client decisions recorded on the client's behalf.

1. Domain (src/domain/change-control.ts):
- `requiredSteps({contractImpact, amountKeur, ccbThresholdKeur})`: PM and director always 'pending'. Client 'pending' unless the impact is 'none', else 'na'. CCB 'pending' when |amount| > threshold (strictly), else 'na'.
- `currentStep(chain)`: the first pending step in pm → director → client → ccb order whose earlier steps are all 'ok' or 'na'.
- Both `canDecideStep` functions (change-control.ts:153 and approvals.ts:236) and `pendingStepFor` also require step === currentStep. `crAwaitsClient` (rexclient.ts:345), Home `crAwaiting` (home.ts:376), client.ts:137 and `listChangeRequestsAwaiting` (changes.ts:181) use currentStep.
- `deriveCrStatus` and `deriveChangeStatus` stay as they are.

2. Data:
- `change_requests.contract_impact`, nullable ('none' | 'price' | 'dates' | 'price_dates'), with a generated migration and the codes in codes.ts.
- Existing-DB backfill in the seed: client 'na' → 'none'; otherwise 'price' when the amount ≠ 0 and 'dates' when the days ≠ 0.
- Seeded decisions are kept as stored.

3. Writers:
- `createChangeRequest` writes the steps from `requiredSteps`, with the threshold read in the same transaction.
- `submitChangeRequest` recomputes the steps of a draft. The seeded CR-009 has amount 0, so its CCB becomes 'na'; the tests deciding CR-009's CCB step change.
- `decideChangeStep` refuses a step that is not current. The three Server Functions map that refusal to a "not yours to decide yet" message.

4. New CR form: a required "Contractual impact" select, validated by `parseNewChangeRequest`.

5. Settings: key "change.ccbThreshold" (k€).
- Default 50 k€, flagged in the notes as the prototype's highlight value until management sets the real one.
- Seeded by both seeds (demo and production), added to the SettingsDefaults document (src/domain/settings-defaults.ts) and restored by Reset.
- Read through a cached reader like health-thresholds.ts.
- Director-only field next to "Project health rules", validated ≥ 0 and audited.

6. A-4 posting: `isContractualChange` = client step ok and the impact includes the price.
- Amendment amount = CR amount, as today (cost-control open question 2 stays open).
- A dates-only change prepares no money amendment; record this as an open question.

7. UI:
- Drawer hint "Waiting for <previous step>".
- The KPI sub-line names the current steps.
- Update the FilterNote and spec 07 line 261 / OQ3.

8. Tests:
- Domain: step order, the n/a rule, the strict threshold boundary.
- DAL: refusal of an out-of-order step; CR-008 reaches the portal and the client's My approvals only after the director approves.
- Rights for the 8 personas, FR strings, golden-test deviation comments citing P-6b.
- Update existing tests: delivery2-writes.test.ts:49-56,74; cost-control.test.ts:127-128; approvals.test.ts:166; db-check.ts:605 (portal decisions become D-014, D-016 only).

Excluded, to the open questions:
- Client portal "approved changes" limited to contractual CRs (cost-control open question 4).
- CCB required on every re-baseline.
- PM recording the client's decision while there are no client accounts (I-2b, R-179).
- Next CCB meeting date (debt-ux open question 6).

Risks: I-2b = c (no client access at launch): in production nobody can record a required client step, so contractual CRs stop after the director and never reach the CCB or approval. The gap already exists today; sequencing does not create it, but it should be stated in DELIVERY-8 §8 and not worked around without the owner. · The requester may pick "None (internal)" to skip the client step. Only the director's approval checks that choice, and the scope has no way to correct it after creation. Note it as an open question (P-6c option b let the director correct). · The 50 k€ default threshold is a value Keystone chose, not management's (waiting_on). Until management sets it in Settings, CRs of 50 k€ or less skip the CCB. A default of 0 would keep today's behaviour (CCB always required) and is a safer alternative to discuss. · Choosing ≥ or > at the threshold changes who decides. P-6c's "au-delà" and the prototype's `amt > 50` both mean strictly greater. · Golden tests compare with the prototype's parallel logic (scripts/proto-golden-rexclient.ts, home and approvals goldens). Each deliberate change needs a comment citing P-6b/P-6c. · Many existing tests and expectations decide the client or CCB step before the director, or rely on the portal showing CR-008: delivery2-writes.test.ts, cost-control.test.ts, approvals.test.ts, myapprovals tests, db-check.ts:605 and the e2e planning scenario (CR approved by PM, client, director and CCB). They all need reordering. · Recomputing the steps of the seeded draft CR-009 at submission makes its CCB step 'na' (amount 0), which changes the existing BL2 approval flow and its tests. Keeping the baseline→CCB rule would avoid that, but P-6c does not ask for that rule. · Reset to defaults reads the stored settings.defaults document (R-170). If the threshold is not added to SettingsDefaults and its parser, Reset will not restore it, and a document stored before this change must fall back to the default value. · A-4 amendment for a dates-only contractual change: no amount is prepared, as today. The owner may expect an amendment for the dates too. This joins cost-control open question 2 (selling price) as open. · Steps are fixed at submission with the threshold in force then. A later change of the threshold does not touch requests already in review, which should be stated in the Settings help text.

#### R-015 — Notifier les événements clés aux personnes concernées (X-6a) (partly buildable, L)

Bell notifications in Keystone only (X-6a), through one recipients helper built on createNotification with toPersonIds. The helper maps persons to their active user accounts, never notifies the actor, and leaves client users to the existing read-time filter (dal/shell.ts:389-405). Every notice is sent after the writer's transaction commits, from the Server Function or job. A failure is logged with logError and never undoes the write.

Live now:
(1) CR submitted (features/changes/actions.ts after submitChangeRequest). Either build R-069 (sequential chain P-6b) first and notify the approver of the first pending step, then the next step's approver after each decision, or, without R-069, notify the approver of every pending step at submit.
- PM step: the PM of record (default from notes-rights §6 Q4).
- Director step: users with the director role.
- Client step: client users of the project's client organisation (none at launch).
- CCB step: the director (P-6a), not a Settings value.
- On final approval or rejection: the requester (requestedByPersonId).
(2) Delivery refused: after recordReceiptQc or rejectReceipt with rejected units > 0, notify the project leads and the PO's creator or submitter, else the procurement users.
(3) Invoice blocked: invoices of the receipt or its PO whose matchedInvoiceStatus turns from 'awaiting_receipt' to 'blocked' after QC (compare before and after). Notify the PO creator or submitter, else the procurement users, plus the director (prototype n8).
(4) @mention (also closes R-149): parse '@First Last' or '@F. Last' in addTaskComment against persons with an active account and notify the person mentioned. Unknown names are ignored.
(5) Task assigned: tasks created from meeting actions (dal/meetings.ts) and activity to-dos (dal/activity-todos.ts) notify the assignee, unless the assignee is the actor. Tasks born from requests already do this.
(6) KPI alert: new job 'kpi-alerts' (hourly) in src/domain/jobs.ts and src/server/jobs/definitions.ts. It runs getPortfolioAlerts('en') with ALERT_RULE_DEFAULTS and the health thresholds in Settings, at the current data date (Friday freeze waits for S-4). A new table, added with a generated migration, records the notified alert keys. A new key notifies once; it can notify again only after it clears and reappears.
- Project alerts go to the project leads and the director.
- Portfolio-wide alerts (over-allocation, approvals waiting) go to the director.
- The first run needs a baseline so it does not flood the bell with every alert already active.

Dormant until Vigie publishes its API (V-3), built as an isolated hook in refreshFromVigie of the HTTP connectors only (diff before and after the upsert, never inside upsertNcrMirror or upsertMirror, which the seed also calls), and covered by tests with injected records:
(7) NCR raised: notify the project leads, plus the quality referent and engineering lead when they are on the project team.
(8) ECO entering in_approval: notify each pending key holder. The pm key goes to the leads of the affected projects (leadProjectCodes); the quality and englead keys go to users holding those roles.

French texts go in src/i18n/extra/d8-notifications.json. Titles are fixed templates; typed text stays in the sub-line.

Tests per event: recipient set, actor excluded, client exclusion, dedup.

Do not mark R-015 'fait' for events (7) and (8) until the Vigie API exists. Update R-149 and R-121 in reste-a-developper.json.

Risks: createNotification opens its own db.transaction (dal/notifications.ts:103-118). Called from inside a writer's transaction (recordReceiptQc, the Vigie refresh transaction) it can block on PGlite's single connection. With R-010 transaction retries it can also send the same notice twice. Emit notifications only after commit. · The bell passes titles through t() (components/app/shell/page-header.tsx:77). A title holding typed text (CR title, project name in a KPI alert, task title) would be segment-translated, against R-148. Keep titles as fixed English templates and put typed text in the sub-line (fieldsText). · The KPI alert job: getPortfolioAlerts works at DEFAULT_DATA_DATE = STATUS_ISO (S-4 open) with code-default thresholds ('Alert thresholds in Settings' is open in DELIVERY-7 §8). Alerts change only when the data changes. The first run would notify every alert already active (Harbor Line etc.) unless a baseline is stored first. · In getPortfolioAlerts, directorQueue takes the identity from PERSONA_BY_ROLE.director. Run from the worker in Entra mode, it may not match the real director's approval queue. · CR routing depends on R-069 (sequential chain P-6b), which is not built. Without it, either notify all pending approvers at once (as the prototype n1 does: director and client) or build R-069 first. Routing the PM step to every PM or deputy is an open question (notes-rights §6 Q4). · NCR and ECO notifications are dead code in mock mode, the only mode that works today. Marking R-015 'fait' would overstate what works; give them a progress note that they wait on Vigie (V-3). · NCR recipients beyond the leads depend on project team roles. The demo referents exist only on Harbor Line, and real teams wait for the owner's RACI matrix. Fall back to the project leads. · No buyer field on purchase_orders: createdBy or submittedBy may be null on seeded POs, so the fallback to all procurement users will be common. · The bell already holds the seeded prototype notifications (n1-n10), so live and demo notices will be mixed in demo databases. · The notification panel footer still advertises an e-mail digest and Teams (R-036, X-6b). It stays wrong until the mail relay exists; this item is out of scope for that.

#### R-081 — Figer le montant des catégories de coûts après BL1 (buildable, M)

The triage scope stands, with these additions and fixes.

1. Domain rule.
   - `categoryAmountFrozen(codes) = !canSnapshotFreely(codes)` goes in src/domain/baseline-policy.ts.
   - It is true once the project has a BLn with n ≥ 1, in any state.

2. Server Functions and DAL.
   - Read the project's baseline codes with `listProjectBaselines` (src/server/dal/baselines.ts).
   - `editCostCategoryAction` and `updateProjectCostCategory` refuse a change of `budgetKeur` once frozen, with "After BL1 the amount changes only through an approved change request".
   - Do the check inside the DAL transaction as well, not only in the Server Function.
   - When frozen, a missing `budget` value must be accepted. Today `parseCategoryBudget(undefined)` returns null, which gives an error. Name and type stay editable.
   - `addProjectCostCategory` after BL1 creates the category at 0 k€ (forecast 0).
   - The director is not exempt.

3. Excel/CSV import (new to the scope).
   - In `planTableImport` / `writeTableImport` for `budget_lines` (src/server/interchange/table-import.ts):
     - a row that would change an existing category's budget after BL1 becomes an error and is skipped, with the same message;
     - a row that creates a category after BL1 creates it at 0, with a warning.
   - Update interchange.test.ts:262-283.

4. UI (setup-tab.tsx, category-forms.ts).
   - When frozen, the Edit and Add dialogs drop the budget field. FormDef has no read-only field.
   - A hint explains the rule and links to Change control `?new=1`.
   - The Budget page passes the frozen flag.

5. The approved-CR path (A-4, R-068).
   - An optional "Cost category" select on the New CR form, listing the project's categories.
   - Shown only to the project team and hidden from the client, like `withActivities` (change-buttons.tsx, changes/page.tsx:80).
   - Validated server-side against the project's categories in `createChangeRequestAction`.
   - Stored in `change_requests.cost_category_code`, with a new migration from `npm run db:generate` (after 0015).
   - Inside the existing idempotent posting, after the `change_request_postings` insert, `postApprovedChange` adds the CR amount to that category's budget. It adds it to the forecast too while the category has no actual and no commitment. This is audited in the Budget area, and the posting's audit line names the category.
   - Guard against a negative amount that would take the category below 0: refuse when the CR is raised, and clamp or refuse at posting. This is a default to document.
   - Without a category, only the BAC and the work packages move, as today. This is a default for the owner to confirm, listed in the delivery's open questions.

6. Tests.
   - The domain rule.
   - Refusal on HLT-026 (BL1 current).
   - Success on a project with no BL1: a project created in the test and rescheduled so it gets `project_controls`, or a test DB with the baselines removed.
   - Add-at-0 after BL1.
   - The import error after BL1.
   - CR posting into a category, including idempotency (posting twice adds once).
   - Update category-actions.test.ts:54-77, which today edits budgets on HLT-026, and any render test of the Edit form.
   - French translations in src/i18n/extra/budget.json and changes.json.

Risks: The triage scope misses the Excel/CSV budget_lines import (table-import.ts:162-189). Without it, the PM or director can still overwrite category budgets after BL1. · Every seeded project with cost data has a current BL1 (seed/chapter4.ts). After the change, budget editing is gone on all demo projects. Existing tests that edit HLT-026 budgets (category-actions.test.ts, interchange.test.ts) have to be rewritten, and the 'free before BL1' test needs a newly created, rescheduled project. · Approved CRs with no category picked make the sum of the categories drift from the BAC. HLT-026's seeded categories add up to the current BAC (4 600 + 200 = 4 800). The 'no category means nothing moves' default needs the owner's confirmation; making the category required for budget CRs is the other option. · A negative CR posted into a small category could take its budget below 0, which validBudget forbids elsewhere. Throwing inside decideChangeStep would block the approval itself, so a guard when the CR is raised and/or a clamp at posting is needed. · Cost categories are internal cost data. The new CR select must stay hidden from the client and be re-checked server-side (same pattern as withActivities and P-10b). · The owner's S-6 note says the project budget breakdown (NIM codes tied to budgets) will be revisited. A new change_requests.cost_category_code column may need to move to NIM codes later (R-082). · Migration-number clash with other delivery-8 lots: regenerate at integration, never edit a generated migration. · editCostCategoryAction currently requires a parseable budget. When the field is dropped from the form, the action and the DAL patch must treat a missing budget as 'unchanged', otherwise every name or type edit after BL1 will fail.

#### R-077 — Bloquer la facturation quand les jalons dépassent 100 % (buildable, M)

The triage scope stands, with these corrections.

1. Rule:
- Add `invoiceBlocked` in src/domain/contract.ts. It is true when the milestone's basis group (base contract, or its amendment) has Σ shares > 100 + 0.01.
- `issueInvoice` recomputes that group's total inside its transaction (the contract row is already locked by loadContract) and refuses with "Payment milestones exceed 100 % — invoicing is blocked until the schedule is corrected".
- `issueInvoiceAction` checks the block before parseInvoiceForm. Otherwise filtering blocked milestones out of `planned` returns the generic "Choose a milestone still to invoice" instead of the reason.
- Update the doc comment of addPaymentMilestone (contract.ts:636-637).

2. Billing screen:
- Disable the header and row "Issue invoice" buttons for blocked milestones and show the reason.
- invoiceFormDef excludes blocked milestones.
- The Contract total hint becomes "Shares total N % · invoicing blocked" when a group is over 100 %. It stays "Shares must total 100 %" when the group is under 100 %. Apply this per group, in both baseTotal/total and the amendment groups' hint (model.ts:259-263).

3. Schedule correction:
- `updatePaymentMilestone`: label, share 0–100, contractual date, condition. Planned milestones only; the basis is not changed.
- `removePaymentMilestone`: planned milestones only, with a confirmation. A hard delete is acceptable because no FK references a planned milestone (customer_invoices cascade only).
- Server Functions with the editable(code,'contract') gate, as for "Add milestone".
- Row actions on the Contract schedule, reusing milestoneFormDef with initial values.
- Both are audited (area Contract, entity payment_milestone). An activity entry is optional: addPaymentMilestone records none today.

4. Tests:
- Domain group rule.
- DAL: refusal at 100.01 %, then success after an edit or removal.
- Server Function rights, including gate.test.ts.
- Update model.test.ts:62 (golden 'milestone' and 'milestone-late', EN and FR) with a deviation that cites C-7. Also update model.test.ts:134 if the To-invoice `line` carries the reason.
- Rewrite portfolio-alerts.test.ts:24-34, which invoices a milestone in a 101 % schedule (for example, lower M5 first or add the milestone at 0 %).
- Check that contract.render.test.ts tolerates the new actions column.

Defaults to record as open questions for the owner:
- Per-group block (alternative: the whole contract).
- Under 100 % stays a hint only.

Risks: The triage's claim that the golden test stays unchanged is false. model.test.ts:62-64 checks the Schedule total hint against the prototype for the 105 % ('milestone') and 102.5 % ('milestone-late') scenarios in EN and FR. Changing the hint breaks 4 golden assertions, which must be updated with a deviation comment citing C-7. The To-invoice golden check (model.test.ts:134) also breaks if the row line changes. · portfolio-alerts.test.ts:24-34 adds a 1 % milestone (base total 101 %) and then calls issueInvoice. The new rule makes it throw, so the test must be rewritten. · If blocked milestones are filtered out of `planned` before parseInvoiceForm, issueInvoiceAction returns 'Choose a milestone still to invoice' instead of the blocking reason. The block must be checked first, or the message mapped. · Hard delete plus nextBusinessNumber: removing the highest-numbered milestone (say M7) lets the next 'Add milestone' reuse M7. The audit history then has two different M7s. Consider not reusing numbers, or soft removal (soft removal would need a migration, since the status enum is planned/invoiced/paid only). · S-5 (cited for the confirmation) also asks to keep the possibility of undo. A plain hard delete with confirmation meets only half of it. · Per-group versus whole-contract blocking is a default. C-7 says 'le total'. DELIVERY-7 §8 lists 'R-077 blocking share check' as open, so the default must be recorded as an open question for the owner. · Editing or removing planned milestones changes the cash-flow forecast, the late-billing badge and the R-130 alerts, the Budget › Cash & recovery figures and the status report. That is expected, but existing tests on those figures may need a check. · Rights: the Finance role named in P-4 does not exist yet. Edit and remove inherit the 'contract' edit right (director, PM and the project lead per R-041), so Finance gets them only when the role is added. · Adding a row-actions column to the Contract payment-milestone table changes the render. contract.render.test.ts and the tablet layouts (R-146) may need adjusting.

#### R-080 — Colorer les indicateurs du Budget selon les seuils et signer les barres d’écart (buildable, S)

The proposed scope stands, with two small additions that the same decision covers.

1. Budget page (src/app/(app)/projects/[code]/budget/page.tsx).
   - Read `healthThresholds()` in the existing Promise.all.
   - Pass `th` to PerformanceTab as an optional prop, defaulting to DEFAULT_HEALTH_THRESHOLDS.

3. Signed bars.

   - Tooltip: f(cv, true).

4. Add-on: `costNote(stored, cpi, th)` in src/server/dal/cost.ts.

   - getCostControl reads the cached `healthThresholds()`.

5. Tests.

   - NDC-031 (CPI ≈ 1.10): no CPI, EAC or VAC alert.
   - A custom-threshold case for both the domain helper and costNote.

Files: performance-tab.tsx, budget.module.css, evm.ts, evm.test.ts, budget.render.test.ts, budget/page.tsx, plus src/server/dal/cost.ts (costNote). health-thresholds.ts needs no change.

Afterwards, update docs/reste-a-developper.json for R-080 according to the standing rule.

Risks: The TCPI rule (tcpi > 1/risk ≈ 1.03) and the EAC/VAC rule (follow CPI) are the triage's own defaults. C-1 says only 'same thresholds as project health'. Isolate them as named constants and list them as an open question in DELIVERY-8 §8. · Other screens would stay inconsistent. The PM Home still hard-codes CPI and Forecast (EAC) as always in alert and SPI against a literal 0.97 (src/domain/home.ts:464-466; pmHome receives no thresholds, src/server/dal/home.ts:257). The Overview's 'Forecast (EAC)' tile is always in alert (src/domain/portfolio.ts:147). After R-080, the same project (e.g. NDC-031, CPI 1.10) would be neutral on Budget but in alert on Home. No remaining-work item tracks the Home tiles. A-13 'toutes les vues lisent les seuils' arguably covers the SPI literal. Fix it alongside, or record it as a new item or open question. · If the Variance card values (CV/SV) and costNote's literal 0.97 are left out, the card stays half-coloured or contradicts the tiles under a custom Settings threshold. · Golden and display tests change on purpose: the evm.test.ts HLT heights and budget.render.test.ts '--bar-h:80%'. Each change must cite C-1 under the golden-test rule. The render-test helper (line 39) builds PerformanceTab props by hand, so the new `th` prop must be optional or the helper updated. · Signed bars in a 110 px area leave about 55 px per side, so small months collapse to the stub. Check legibility on a tablet (R-146) and the axis in light and dark themes (mono-accent design: no second alert colour). · The owner rated C-1 'Plus tard' (later). It is buildable but should not displace items marked 'Prochaine étape' / 'À traiter en priorité' when delivery 8 is planned.

#### R-054 — Lier le livrable « Planning BL1 posé » à la référence BL1 réelle (buildable, S)

The scope is as proposed, with these precisions.
1. Domain (baseline-policy.ts):
   - BL1_STAGE_DELIVERABLE = { stage: 'apd', position: 1 } (0-based, « Planning BL1 posé »).
   - hasBl1(baselines): true when a baseline numbered ≥ 1 is current or superseded; proposed ones do not count.
2. Transaction-aware writer markBl1Deliverable(tx, projectId, actor):
   - Upserts done=true with doneAt and doneByPersonId.
   - Returns whether the state changed. Return this from the transaction callback rather than setting an outer variable, because transactions may run more than once (R-010).
   - Writes the audit and activity line only when the state changed.
   - Call it in createBaselineSnapshot and snapshotIntoBaseline when makeCurrent produces a BLn with n ≥ 1.
   - Call it in both branches of the change-request approval in changes.ts: the empty snapshot filled at approval (:311-313) and the already-snapshotted direct update (:314-317).
   - Call it in writeBaselines of the NEW import when BL1 is written current.
   - No auto-advance, whatever the stage.
3. Plan « Set baseline » and Schedule « Re-baseline » toasts add « · “Planning BL1 posé” ticked » only when the tick changed. This keeps scheduling-actions.test.ts:81 unchanged.
4. Manual tick:
   - Ticking APD#1 without BL1 is refused, and unticking it while BL1 exists is refused, in setDeliverableAction and setDeliverableAndAdvance.
   - The message should not imply one click: a project with no baselines gets BL0 on its first « Set baseline », so say « Set BL1 first (Plan › Set baseline, up to BL1) — or force the stage change ».
   - The forced move is unchanged.
5. Register popover:
   - APD#1 is read-only, with a hint, using a `bl1` flag (and the BL1 setOn date) computed in getRegisterData.
   - Keep asGolden unchanged.
6. Wizard text: change the EN source string and the FR string (fr-dict.json or an extra i18n file).
7. Tests:
   - Plan BL1 at Initiative or APD ticks APD#1; BL0 does not.
   - CR approval ticks APD#1, in both the empty and the snapshotted case.
   - The NEW MS Project import ticks APD#1.
   - The manual tick is refused without BL1; the untick is refused with BL1.
   - The forced APD→Études move still works without BL1.
   - The register goldens are unchanged.
8. No migration and no data fix.
9. In the delivery-8 notes, record DELIVERY-6 §8 L-2 as closed by the answer's option detail.

Risks: Owner-decision traceability: DELIVERY-6 §8 still lists L-2 as open, and DELIVERY-7 §8 says earlier unrepeated questions stay open. The chosen option text settles it, but the delivery-8 notes should say explicitly that it is closed. Otherwise a reviewer may see the tick as built ahead of an answer. · No auto-advance: when the other two APD items are already ticked, setting BL1 completes APD without moving the project. The register help says « When every deliverable is ticked the project moves to the next stage automatically », so the PM sees 3/3 at APD and must use « Move to » (auto mode is accepted). This is a default; the prototype's toggleDeliv auto-advances. · The first « Set baseline » on a project with no baselines (wizard projects) creates BL0, not BL1 (nextBaselineCode). The PM must set a baseline twice before the item ticks. The refusal message and the register hint must make this clear. · Rights: « Set baseline » is open to schedule editors and CR approval to the approval chain. Either can tick a lifecycle deliverable, which the register otherwise reserves to the project leads (mayManageLifecycle). This is intended by L-2, but the audit line must name the real actor. · Transactions may run more than once (R-010): the « ticked » flag for the toast must come back as the callback's return value, never through an outer variable. The upsert is idempotent; the audit and activity lines must be written only on a state change, so a retried transaction does not log them twice. · Missing a call site: the changes.ts approval has two branches (empty snapshot filled at approval, and an existing snapshot made current directly), and the import has two modes (only 'new' makes BL1 current). If one is missed, the system becomes inconsistent: BL1 exists, the item is unticked, and only a manual tick fixes it. · Existing databases (the user's ./.data, earlier demos) may already hold an APD project with APD#1 ticked by hand and no BL1. Without a data fix, the item stays ticked, auto passage is still accepted, and only an untick-then-retick shows the new rule. · An NEW import whose file has only Baseline 1 (no Baseline 0) creates BL2 superseded with no current baseline. hasBl1 is then true but nothing was ticked automatically. A manual tick is still allowed, so this is acceptable but worth a test or a comment. · Toast change: if the suffix is appended on every upsert instead of only on a real change, scheduling-actions.test.ts:81 (NDC-031, APD#1 already ticked) and possibly the Schedule re-baseline toasts break.

#### R-140 — Show the CR justification in the CR drawer and to the client (X-11) (buildable, S)

Items (1) to (6) are kept as proposed, with these corrections:

(1) Add `justification?: string | null` to ChangeRequestLike (src/domain/change-control.ts) and to the portal's DecisionRow, filled by crDecisionRow from cr.justification (src/domain/rexclient.ts). Deliverable rows set it to null. The text is passed through raw.

(2) In CrDrawerBody (src/features/changes/changes-view.tsx), add a "Justification" section after "Raised by": an Eyebrow plus a pre-wrap paragraph rendered with dataText(cr.justification, lang), never t(). Omit it when the text is null or blank. Every viewer of the drawer sees it, the client included, as X-11 = shared requires.

(3) On the client portal (src/features/rexclient/model.ts and client-view.tsx DecisionsTab), show the justification under the sub line of each change request waiting for the client:
- The line reads "Why: …", with dataText applied to the text only, not to the label.
- It is clamped to 3 lines with a title attribute or a details toggle, styled in rexclient.module.css.
- Decided rows are unchanged.

(4) On the New CR form's 'why' field (src/features/changes/forms.ts), set `placeholder: 'Shown to the client with the change request'`, which FormDialog already translates. If a real hint line under the field is wanted, it also needs a `hint` property in src/components/app/form-def.ts and its rendering in src/components/app/FormDialog.tsx, which is not in the original file list.

(5) Add the FR strings: the drawer strings in src/i18n/extra/changes.json, the portal strings ("Why", the clamp/toggle labels) in src/i18n/extra/rexclient.json. Reuse the existing 'Justification' key in fr-dict.json.

(6) Tests:
- Domain: crDecisionRow carries the justification, and deliverable rows carry null.
- Drawer render (changes.render.test.ts): the section appears when a justification is set and is absent for the seeded CRs.
- Portal render (rexclient render.test.ts): the same, for a waiting CR that has a justification.
- A typed justification is shown as typed in FR (R-148).
- The client's existing R-043 checks stay green (no plan-activities editor for the client, approvals and export unchanged).

No schema change, migration or DAL change is needed.

Risks: Form hint: FormFieldDef has no `hint`, so the proposed hint needs either the field's placeholder or a change to the shared FormDialog and form-def (more files, and it affects every form). A placeholder disappears once the user starts typing, so the reminder to write with the client in mind is weak. · Inline approval on My approvals: the client (and the internal approvers) can approve or reject a CR inline from My approvals (src/domain/approvals.ts changeRequestItems, sub line = type, amount and days only) without seeing the justification. Only opening the drawer through the row's href shows it. Extending the My approvals sub line is not in this scope and would be a separate, small choice. · Internal CRs: a seeded 'na' client step (CR-007) means the client sees internal CRs on Change control. With the justification shown to every viewer, a future internal CR's justification would reach the client. Today this does not happen: every CR created in Keystone gets a pending client step, and seeded CRs carry no justification. Revisit this if the owner answers open question #4 in notes-cost-control.md §8 (internal CRs shown to the client), or if a 'client step not required' path is added. · No edit after creation: the text is written once, and the PM cannot reword it before the CR reaches the client. Existing CRs created before the form hint was added may contain free internal wording that becomes visible to the client. This follows from X-11 = shared, but the owner should be told. · R-148: the justification must go through dataText/exactText and never through t(). In the portal model, `sub` currently goes through t(d.sub); the new field must not be added to that string. · Seeded and prototype CRs have no justification, so on the demo data the feature is visible only after a new CR is created. The render tests must build a fixture with a justification, and the e2e tests or a demo will not show it on HLT-026 by default. · Once implemented, docs/reste-a-developper.json (R-140 progress, history, updated) must be updated and the remaining-work page republished, per the owner's standing rule.

Rights via getProjectContext / projectAccessResolver / getScreenAccess (ctx.isLead, ctx.canEdit, refuseClient); each Server Function validates input and permission server-side, writes through DAL writers in one transaction (re-read state inside it, return values from the callback), audits, records activity, revalidatePath. Never UPDATE/DELETE audit_entries. Never edit docs/reste-a-developper.json: write docs/delivery-8/notes-supply-engineering.md. Insert new code next to related code, never at the end of a shared file. Hand over green: typecheck, lint, test, db:check.

### Item scopes (triage, after the adversarial check)

#### R-095 — Cancel a purchase order with a reason (draft now; placed order after arbitration) (partly buildable, M)

Build now: cancel before the order is placed (SC-3a = a and P-5c = a agree).
(1) Domain rule canCancelPo in src/domain/orders.ts:
- Draft: the author (createdByPersonId) may cancel. The project's leads (isLead) and the director may also cancel, as a stated default that goes beyond SC-3a.
- Awaiting approval: the submitter may withdraw. Approvers keep Reject.
- Only orders drafted in Keystone (poX3State === 'local', origin shortfall or rfq). X3-origin orders are not cancellable in Keystone.
- Never once Ordered.
(2) A new migration via npm run db:generate adding cancel_reason, cancelled_by_person_id and cancelled_at to purchase_orders.
(3) DAL writer cancelPurchaseOrder:
- re-reads the status and origin inside the transaction;
- requires a reason, trimmed, at most 1000 characters;
- sets the status to cancelled;
- writes an audit row "status: Draft|Awaiting approval → Cancelled · <reason>" and an activity line.
rejectPurchaseOrder takes and stores a reason.
(4) Server Function cancelOrderAction (Orders editable plus the role rule, checked server-side) and revalidatePath. decidePurchaseOrderAction accepts the reason.
(5) UI:
- an Orders row or drawer "Cancel order" button with a reason dialog;
- the drawer of a cancelled order shows "Cancelled <date> · <who> · <reason>";
- the My approvals PO Reject goes through the existing RejectDialog with a required reason (add a 'purchase-order' case to rejectPrompt);
- the internal PO PDF shows the cancellation;
- the client never sees it (no Orders screen for the client already).
(6) Optional: a notification to the author or submitter through createNotification({ toPersonIds }), when someone else cancels or rejects. No answer asks for it, so keep it best-effort.
(7) RFQ footnote: rfqHint and the procurement model show the drafted order's status (Cancelled) instead of the fixed "purchase order created as draft". This needs src/domain/procurement.ts and src/features/procurement/model.ts.
(8) French i18n strings. Tests for actions, the domain or model, the reject reason, and the refusal on X3-origin and placed orders. Golden updates cite SC-3a/P-5c only if a displayed value changes.
Blocked:
- Cancel on Ordered, Partially received and Received orders, including Keystone-drafted orders approved but not pushed: SC-3a and P-5c contradict each other there, and no X3 connector or outbox exists (I-3b is "à discuter").
- "Solder le reste à livrer" (SC-3a only).
- Cancelling X3-origin orders and reporting cancellations to Sage X3.

Risks: All seeded orders are origin 'x3', including PO-1082 and PO-5141, which await approval. If cancel or withdraw is not limited to Keystone-drafted orders, Keystone diverges from X3, and a later daily X3 import (the I-3b proposal) could bring the order back or overwrite it. The existing Reject already has this gap. · Letting project leads and the director cancel a draft goes beyond SC-3a, which names only the author. Record it as a default in the delivery notes and ch4-defaults so the owner can reverse it. · P-5a is pending: the owner's purchasing procedure defines the approval workflow in Sage. Who approves, rejects or withdraws may change once it is received, so keep the rule in one domain function. · A cancelled order leaves the committed figures (isCommittedPo counts orders awaiting approval), so KPIs, orders placed and EVM move when an order is withdrawn. Tests that cancel seeded data must not change the golden baselines. · rfqHint currently says 'purchase order created as draft' whatever the order's status. Without touching procurement, an RFQ whose draft was cancelled shows a misleading footnote, and its link opens a Cancelled order. · A notification is not asked for by any answer. Keep it best-effort, outside the write transaction, so a notification failure cannot undo the cancellation. It may need a new NotificationKind. · Drafts from the BOM shortfall assistant come back into the shortfall when cancelled, so 'Create draft POs' recreates them under new numbers. This is expected, but the draft numbering question (Q-7/R-014) is still open. · Both the Orders row action and the My approvals decision must re-check the status in the transaction. Otherwise a withdrawal can race with an approval (R-010 concurrent edits is still open).

#### R-034 — Construire le connecteur Sage X3 (extractions quotidiennes et envois) (partly buildable, S)

Make the Sage X3 mock consistent with A-15 and with R-021 as built. Do not build the connector itself.

1) Keep the Sage X3 row of Activity & integrations as Delivery 7 left it: 'Not connected' and no 'Sync now', on demo and production databases alike. Leave connectorLink alone. If the team still wants 'Mock' on the demo database, it must also define what 'Sync now · Sage X3' does (for example, run the stock import), stop the row showing the seeded volume, and update tests/e2e/01-platform.spec.ts and the R-021 unit tests.

2) Demo database (`databaseSeedMode` = demo): 'Import from X3', the stock drawer's 'Push to X3' and the PO drawer's 'Push to X3' keep working. Their toasts follow the PDM wording, e.g. 'Simulated import (Sage X3 not connected) · N lines · M local changes kept' and 'Simulated push (Sage X3 not connected) · …', with EN source strings, the FR dictionaries and the matching FR_RULES. The audit and activity rows also say simulated.

3) Production database: hide the three buttons (src/app/(app)/purchasing/stock/page.tsx primary, src/features/stock/stock-drawer.tsx, src/features/orders/order-drawer.tsx via `view.canPush`). Refuse them server-side in the Server Functions and, as a second check, in the DAL writers `importStockFromX3`, `pushStockToX3` and `pushPurchaseOrderToX3` ('Sage X3 is not connected'). No snapshot line, `x3_sync_runs` row, `pushedToX3At` or fake `X3-…` reference then reaches real data.

4) Production wording. The order drawer's Sage X3 line (src/features/orders/model.ts:193), the stock KPI 'Local changes · To push back to X3' and the drawer note 'Local movements are flagged until the next push to X3' change to a 'Sage X3 not connected' wording instead of promising a push.

5) Optional, same D-7a pattern as Codex, Vigie and Cursus: also refuse the demo mock when IT disables 'Sage X3' in the connectors file (`connectorEnabled('Sage X3')`).

6) Keep planX3Import as the landing point of the future stock extract. In the lot notes, record where the daily 'x3-import' job goes (src/domain/jobs.ts JOB_SCHEDULES, once a day per I-3b) and the list of extracts expected from the owner. Do not register the job, because it would only re-import the prototype snapshot.

Tests: demo toasts and audit say simulated; production refuses all three actions and the buttons are not rendered; the existing stock, order and db-check expectations on the demo database stay unchanged.

Still blocked: the X3 extract parsers, the supplier list (R-093), outbound flows and the Keystone / X3 role split (A-1a, P-5a, S-6), 'Paid' supplier invoices (SC-2), and cancelling a placed order in X3 (R-095).

Risks: Turning Sage X3 into 'Mock' on the demo database (triage part 1) reverses part of R-021 as delivered. It breaks tests/e2e/01-platform.spec.ts:78/83 and the admin KPI tests ('1 connected · 3 mock · 5 not connected'). It offers a 'Sync now · Sage X3' that does nothing and shows the seeded prototype volume. Follow the PDM precedent instead. · On a production database, Stock then has no source of lines at all: no X3 import, no manual line creation, and the imports are off by D-7b. The screen stays empty until the real extract exists (R-034, I-3b). Say so in the delivery notes so beta testers on a production-seeded database are not surprised (I-1a). · Gating by seed mode follows the data, not the environment, as intended in R-011. A demo database deployed by mistake to a shared server would still run the simulated X3 actions, now clearly labelled. · A-1a is still open. If the owner picks option b or c (X3 enters, or stock lives only in X3), the stock movement form and PO drafting will change later. This fix must not grow into any outbound design. · Toast and audit wording changes ripple into src/features/stock/actions.test.ts, src/features/orders/actions.test.ts, the delivery-4/5 DAL tests, FR_RULES and scripts/db-check.ts if audit texts are asserted there. Keep the demo data counts unchanged. · Adding an 'x3-import' entry to JOB_SCHEDULES now would make the worker re-import the prototype snapshot every day. Document the slot only and register no job.

#### R-102 — Mettre en place la validation des articles par l'ingénierie (partly buildable, M)

Construisible maintenant : les points 1 à 3 du triage, tels quels.
(1) Dans le tiroir partagé src/features/plm/part-drawer.tsx, bouton « Compléter la fiche » : code HS, restriction export confirmée (« Aucune » explicite ou texte + famille exportControl), délai en semaines (entier ≥ 0), référence fabricant. Le bouton est ouvert aux éditeurs du PLM (getScreenAccess('plm').canEdit : directeur, PM, responsable ingénierie, qualité, achats) tant que l'article est Non validé ; une fois l'article Validé, seuls le responsable ingénierie et le directeur y ont accès. Écriture par un nouveau writer updatePartQualification (src/server/dal/parts.ts), avec audit en zone PLM.
(2) Bouton « Valider l'article », réservé à viewer.role 'englead' ou 'director' sur un article Non validé. La règle pure partValidationErrors (src/domain/parts.ts) teste « != null » et non la véracité, pour que le délai 0 soit accepté. Le writer recontrôle le statut dans la transaction, enregistre validated_at et validated_by_person_id, écrit l'audit « status: Not validated → Validated », puis renvoie un toast et appelle revalidatePath.
(3) Aucun chemin manuel vers Périmé ou Obsolète, ni de retour à Non validé.
Nouvelle migration via npm run db:generate (validated_at, validated_by_person_id, export_reviewed, éventuellement hs_not_applicable), traductions fr-dict, tests des Server Functions pour les 8 personas, de la saisie mal formée et de l'état périmé, et test unitaire de la règle.
(4) Crochet ECO : uniquement comme placeholder isolé et inerte. Il comprend le champ optionnel partStatusChanges dans VigieEcoRecord, marqué TODO(vigie) et V-2, et son application dans upsertMirror quand l'ECO est « released ». Il doit être idempotent : il n'écrit que si le statut diffère, ignore les références inconnues et n'enregistre aucun audit en double lors des rafraîchissements complets avec prune. Ni le mock ni le seed ne le renseignent. Son effet réel attend le module ECO de Vigie (NEW-VIGIE-1) et le contrat V-2. Le formulaire « Nouvel article » reste minimal (E-1b a).

Risks: R-101 / A-1b : si SolidWorks 3DX devient maître des numéros d'articles, « Nouvel article » deviendra une reprise en lecture, et le cycle de vie 3DX (Released/Obsolete) pourra concurrencer le statut Keystone. Il faut garder la validation Keystone comme qualification achats et douane, et isoler le writer de statut. · null a deux sens dans le schéma actuel : hsCode null veut dire à la fois « n/a » et « non renseigné », exportRestriction null « None » et « non renseigné », leadWeeks null « 0 / — » et « non renseigné » (le seed convertit lead 0 en null pour IT-SW-ERP-L). Sans indicateur explicite (export_reviewed, et un équivalent pour un HS « Sans objet »), la règle ne peut pas distinguer ces cas. · Le « Sans objet pour un service » du triage ne correspond à aucun type : PartType n'a pas de 'service', et PartFamily (formulaire Nouvel article) n'a pas de 'software'. Un article créé dans Keystone ne peut donc jamais être de type logiciel. Il faut soit un indicateur HS « sans objet » explicite, soit limiter l'exception au type software (articles du seed et des imports seulement). · Valeur par défaut d'export_reviewed pour les lignes existantes : à false, 3 des 4 articles Non validés du seed (FD-DET-OPT2, HST-BRK-DSC, CTL-WIND-SN, restriction « None ») devront être confirmés avant validation ; EL-BAT-LI-200 porte un texte de restriction et passe. Choix à documenter dans les notes du lot. · Le directeur parmi les valideurs est un défaut : E-1a ne nomme que le responsable ingénierie. Même chose pour la fiche verrouillée une fois Validé. À lister dans les défauts de la livraison 8. En mode Entra, il faut tester viewer.role (rôle actif d'un utilisateur multi-rôles, R-039). · Les imports (src/server/interchange/table-import.ts:240) peuvent écrire `status` directement, ce qui contourne E-1a et E-1b. C'est sans effet aujourd'hui (IMPORTS_ENABLED coupé, D-7b), mais à traiter à leur réouverture, par exemple en forçant not_validated ou en refusant la colonne. · Le crochet ECO étend upsertMirror, documenté comme « seul writer des tables miroir », à l'écriture du catalogue parts. Il est rejoué à chaque rafraîchissement complet et n'est exercé que par des tests unitaires (le mock ne rafraîchit jamais, le seed n'a pas le champ). Le contrat est inventé tant que Vigie n'a pas publié le sien, d'où un risque de réécriture. · Concurrence (R-010 non résolu) : deux éditions simultanées de la fiche ne sont pas détectées. Seul le contrôle du statut dans la transaction de validation protège la transition. · Les tests de page PLM (src/features/plm/page.test.ts) et la vérification Playwright du tiroir peuvent changer avec les nouveaux boutons. Les valeurs golden et le KPI « Not validated » du seed ne bougent pas.
